#### Decisions

| # | Decision | Where |
|---|---|---|
| D-W8B5-301 | **The abstract class becomes a trait plus a handle.** `trait DialogContent` holds every `abstract`/`virtual` member of the C# `NativeDialog` (26 methods, defaults as the C#); `NativeDialog` is `Rc<DialogInner>` and carries every non-virtual member. Each hook gets `&NativeDialog` (the C# `this`) so it can call the `protected` helpers (`place`, `item`, `set_item_text`, ...), which are `pub` methods of the handle (Rust has no `protected`). A content object must NOT store a `NativeDialog` (the handle owns the content: a cycle); a dialog type that has methods for its owner is `struct X { native: NativeDialog, content: Rc<XContent> }` (precedent: D-W8B3H-18). All trait methods are `&self`; state in `Cell`/`RefCell` (the dialog procedure is re-entered by every `SendMessage`, D-W8B4I-1: no borrow is held across an OS call, a hook or a callback). | 18h..18k, 18e, 18o..18t, 19j..19m |
| D-W8B5-302 | **The four slices follow METHOD boundaries, not the plan's line numbers.** The plan cut `NativeDialog.cs` every 365 lines, which splits `ModelessFilter`/`RunLoop` and puts the DPI code in the "owner-draw" slice. Real cut (see the roster): 18h = fields, hooks, `ShowModal`/`ShowModeless`/`EndModal`/`RunLoop`, the dialog procedure and its router; 18i = fonts, dialog units, DPI change, the layout helpers and `PlaceInitially`; 18j = `InitializeDialog`, commands, notifications, scrolling, the list view header hook, tooltips; 18k = owner-draw, `WM_CTLCOLOR*`, wait cursor, `Cleanup`, `WM_CLOSE`. The plan's module names are kept (`core, modal, layout, dpi, focus, events, draw, result`) plus one more file, `tooltip` (18j: 150 lines of C#, its own `unsafe` area). `dialog/mod.rs` is final (lead-owned index), `native/mod.rs` got the line `pub mod dialog;`. Every file declares ITS state struct (`ModalState`, `MetricsState`, `ControlsState`, `EventsState`, `TipState`, `DrawState`) and `core.rs` declares `DialogInner` with all of them (precedent D-W8B4M-1), so no unit edits another unit's struct. | 18h..18k |
| D-W8B5-303 | **18h..18k are ported in ONE PR (sequential commits h, i, j, k) by the same Sonnet porter, then verified as one.** Reason: the dialog procedure (18h) calls members of 18i, 18j and 18k, so no native test of any slice can pass before the last slice lands (the stubs are `todo!()`); splitting would leave three PRs with red or skipped native tests. Each slice still has its own contract, owned files, pure tests and Done-when. Plan edges to add (code edges): 18h also needs 18f (`CommonControlsInit::ensure`, `ListBoxControl::text_at`); 18j also needs 18e (`edit_centering::attach`), 18g (`ListViewControl::{header, draw_header_item}`), 18f (`ListBoxControl::item_from_point`); 18k also needs 18f (`ListBoxControl::draw_item`); all four need 11a..11d, 13c (`message_loop`, `pending_panic`), 13b (`host::icons::apply`, merged), `shell_input::native::{signed_low_word, signed_high_word}` (merged). 18l..18n implement `chrome::ChromePainter` (D-W8B5-202) and have no edge into the base. Downstream: 18e (`impl DialogContent for ListDialog`), 18o..18t, 19j..19m. | plan |
| D-W8B5-304 | **The `GCHandle` is a boxed `Rc` cookie.** `make_cookie(&Rc<DialogInner>)` boxes a clone (`Box<Rc<DialogInner>>`, a thin pointer) and returns it as `isize`: it is the `lpInitParam` of `CreateDialogIndirectParam`, the `DWLP_USER` value and the `ref_data` of the subclass procedures (`self_ref_data()`). Every procedure calls `from_cookie` FIRST, which clones the `Rc`, so a handler may free its own cookie (`cleanup` inside the procedure). `cleanup` frees the cookie last but one (after the subclasses are removed and the window is destroyed, before the `closed` callback). `DWLP_USER` is zeroed before `DestroyWindow`, so the messages of the destruction find no dialog. The cookie keeps a modeless dialog alive after its owner dropped the handle (the C# `GCHandle` did the same). | 18h, 18j, 18k |
| D-W8B5-305 | **Default chrome.** C# `DarkDialogChrome.Instance` needs the production painter, which does not exist before 18l..18n (D-W8B5-202). `core::default_chrome()` returns a per-thread shared `Rc<DarkDialogChrome>` built with `NoPainter`, a private `ChromePainter` that answers `false` to everything; when 18l..18n merge, the lead (or the 18n porter) replaces that one line by the production painter. Tests pass their own recorder with `NativeDialog::with_chrome(content, Some(chrome))` (the C# `RecordingChrome` idea). | 18h, 18l..18n |
| D-W8B5-306 | **Errors and panics.** `show_modal` and `show_modeless` return `Result<_, DialogError>` (`AlreadyShown`, `Template(TemplateError)`, `CreateFailed(Win32Error)`, `Loop(HostError)`) instead of throwing. A panic in a hook or a callback is NOT an error: the dialog procedure `catch_unwind`s it, `pending_panic::capture`s it (first wins), ends the dialog as Cancel and returns 0 (C# `catch { s_pending ??= ...; dialog?.EndModal(IdCancel); return 0; }`); `show_*` calls `rethrow_pending()` after `CreateDialogIndirectParam` and again at the end, so the panic reaches the caller with its original payload (D-W8B3H-4, D-W8B5-207). The C# private `[ThreadStatic] s_pending` is replaced by the shared `host::pending_panic` slot (the same first-wins rule). `AssertUnwindSafe` is allowed around the hook calls (the dialog is closed after a panic, nothing reuses a half-updated state). | 18h..18k |
| D-W8B5-307 | **`finally` is a `Drop` guard.** `show_modal` creates `CleanupGuard(&self)` right after the cookie, whose `Drop` runs `cleanup()`; so `cleanup` also runs while a panic unwinds out of `ready` or out of `rethrow_pending` (the C# `try/finally`). `cleanup` runs `on_closed` under `pending_panic::guarded` (a panic inside a `Drop` during unwinding would abort). | 18h, 18k |
| D-W8B5-308 | **Loop and callback types.** `show_modal(owner, ready: Option<&dyn Fn(&NativeDialog)>, message_loop: Option<&Rc<MessageLoop>>) -> Result<i32, DialogError>`; `show_modeless(owner, message_loop: Option<Rc<MessageLoop>>, ready, closed: Option<Box<dyn FnOnce()>>) -> Result<(), DialogError>` (the loop is stored for the filter removal). `ready` may panic (tests assert inside it, as the C# kit does): the panic propagates through the guard. | 18h |
| D-W8B5-309 | **`ModelessFilter` holds a `Weak<DialogInner>`** (the loop holds the filter: D-W8B3H-18); it implements `MessageFilter` (`pre_filter_message` = `false`, `as_raw` = `Some(self)`) and `RawMessageFilter` (copy the `Msg`, `is_dialog_message`). `cleanup` removes the filter by its `MessageFilterId`. | 18h, 18k |
| D-W8B5-310 | **`items()` is called once per phase** (template build, `WM_INITDIALOG`); the C# getter was called three times on a constant list. A content whose list changes between the phases is a bug of that content. | 18h, 18j |
| D-W8B5-311 | **Notification structures are typed `windows` structs read from the `lParam` under a SAFETY comment**, never byte offsets: `NMHDR` (`hwndFrom` 0, `idFrom` 8, `code` 16), `NMCUSTOMDRAW` (`dwDrawStage` 24, `hdc` 32), `NMTTDISPINFOW` (`lpszText` 24), `TTTOOLINFOW` (written with `cbSize = TTTOOLINFOW_V2_SIZE` = 64 although the SDK struct is 72 bytes), `DRAWITEMSTRUCT` (`CtlID` 4, `itemID` 8, `hwndItem` 24, 64 bytes), `MINMAXINFO` (`ptMinTrackSize` 24), `RECT`. A `size_of`/`offset_of` assertion for each used field sits next to its use (precedent D-W8B5-212). A null `lParam` (impossible from the OS, a crash in the C#) answers `0`/`false`/`None` (Rust-only guard, not a behaviour difference). | 18i..18k |
| D-W8B5-312 | **Arithmetic of the layout code is `i32` wrapping, truncating division, ties-even rounding.** Dialog units `(units * avg + 2) / 4` and `(units * line + 4) / 8`, the header font `height * 14 / 10`, the button row, `PlaceInitially`: `wrapping_*` (the C# default unchecked context), `/` as is, `f64::round_ties_even` for `Math.Round(9.0 * dpi / 72.0)`. Each formula is a pure `pub fn` (`scale_dlu_x`, `scale_dlu_y`, `average_char_width`, `effective_dpi`, `fallback_font_height`, `header_font_height`, `button_width_for`, `button_row_total`, `button_row_positions`, `work_area`, `initial_frame`) tested without a window. | 18i |
| D-W8B5-313 | **Oracle command `dialog-layout`** (`tools/oracle-cs/Commands/W8-18i.cs`, inputs `tests/golden-inputs/dialog-layout.json`, 137 cases): reflection over the internal `NativeDialog` and its subclasses `ListDialog`, `DiagnosticsDialog`, `BenchmarkDialog`, `ZoomPromptDialog` of the `PhotoReview` assembly. A window-less dialog is `RuntimeHelpers.GetUninitializedObject(typeof(ListDialog))` with the private `_avgCharWidth`, `_lineHeight`, `_childHosts` set by reflection. Kinds: `dlu` (112 rows: `Dx`, `Dy`), `button_row` (20 rows: `ButtonRowWidth`, `ButtonWidth`, `PlaceButtonRow` with no live control, so `ButtonWidth` is `Dx(50)`), `style` (4 rows: private `DialogStyle()`/`DialogExStyle()` of the four dialog types), `constants` (1 row: `IdOk`, `IdCancel`, `SizeMinimized`, `WsDefaultDialog`, `LV35`, `WT35`, `IdcWait`). The `style` and `constants` rows are also compared by 18h and 18j. Lead actions: add `// Commands\W8-18i` and `"dialog-layout" => Commands.W818iDialogLayoutCases(inputs),` to `Program.cs`; run the oracle (`tests/golden/dialog-layout.json`); add `| dialog-layout | W8-18i NativeDialog | Dx, Dy, button row, style words, constants |` to `tests/golden-inputs/README.md`. No csproj change. | 18h, 18i, 18j |
| D-W8B5-314 | **Subclass procedures of the base use `pending_panic::guarded` too** (extends D-W8B5-207): the list view header hook (`LV35`) and the item tip hook (`WT35`) are cosmetic in the C# (`catch (Exception) { }`), in Rust a panic is captured, the message still goes to `def_subclass_proc`, and the owner of the native call resumes the panic. Both are removed in `cleanup` BEFORE the cookie is freed (their `ref_data` is the cookie) and on `WM_NCDESTROY`. | 18j, 18k |
| D-W8B5-315 | **Native test conventions of the base.** All `#[ignore = "native: needs desktop"]`; each test runs on its own STA thread (`fixture::on_sta`, `CoInitializeEx` apartment-threaded, joined with a 60 s bound, the panic of the body is resumed), creates its own owner window (a visible `STATIC` popup 640 x 480 at (100, 100)) and simulates the user inside `ready` (the dialog manager, `SendMessage`/`IsDialogMessage`, no fixed sleeps), as the C# `NativeDialogTestKit`. The shared fixture `tests/common/dialogs_dialog_fixture.rs` (helpers of the kit, `RecordingChrome`, the configurable `RecordingContent`) is written by the 18h test porter with the complete spec of `contracts/W8-18h.md` and only READ by the others. The tests that need the whole chain are in the file of the unit that owns the last piece (18k); the lead runs `--include-ignored` once on an interactive session. | 18h..18k |
| D-W8B5-316 | **Who writes `impl DialogContent for ListDialog` (amends D-W8B5-208).** The trait is in the compile-checked stub, so the W8-18e porter writes `impl DialogContent for ListDialog` in `list_dialog.rs` as part of 18e (against the stub, even before 18h is ported: it compiles). `impl ListDialogBase for NativeDialog` is 18i's (`layout.rs`, in the stub). The e2e file `dialogs_native_list_dialog_e2e.rs` stays 18k's. Lead action: amend open question 1 of `contracts/W8-18e.md` accordingly. | 18e, 18i, 18k |
| D-W8B5-317 | **C# helper types that have no Rust twin.** `ShellIcons.Apply` is `host::icons::apply`; `DialogConstants`, `WindowMessages`, `ControlConstants` are `win32::{dialog_native, messages, control_constants}`; `PendingMinMaxInfo`/`Rect`/`Point` are the `windows` structs behind `win32::structs`; `MonitorInfo` is `structs::MonitorInfo`; `ExceptionDispatchInfo` is `pending_panic`; `GCHandle` is the cookie; `NativeMemory.Alloc` of the tip buffer is a `Vec<u16>`; `Func<int, string?>` is `Rc<dyn Fn(i32) -> Option<String>>` (`ItemTipProvider`). | 18h..18k |
| D-W8B5-318 | **C# tests that are not ported here.** `NativeDialogLoopTests` (4 tests) drive `Win32SecondaryWindowPort` and belong to W8-18o; the base-level twins (dispatcher work and message filters keep running inside the nested loop, `WM_QUIT` reposted with the same exit code, close from a queued callback) are native tests of 18h. `NativeDialogSnapshotTests` and `Wp27VisualCheckTests` write screenshots for a human (no assertions): not ported (visual review is a manual step of the desktop run). `NativeDialogChromeTests` (779 lines) tests the painter: W8-18l..18n. | 18h, 18o |
| D-W8B5-319 | **Tooltip ownership.** The tooltip window is an OWNED popup of the dialog (`CreateWindowEx(WS_EX_TOPMOST, ..., WS_POPUP | ..., hWndParent = dialog)`; a popup with a parent handle is owned, not a child), so the OS destroys it with the dialog; `cleanup` only forgets the handle. The `tip_buffer` pointer given to the tooltip is replaced only inside `provide_tip_text`, on the UI thread. | 18j, 18k |
| D-W8B5-320 | **Plan "Different"/Rust-only rows.** (a) `DialogError` instead of exceptions; (b) null-`lParam` guards (D-W8B5-311); (c) the list view / tip subclass panic is resumed instead of swallowed (D-W8B5-314); (d) `items()` once per phase (D-W8B5-310); (e) the property/class prefixes are not relevant here (the base registers no class and no window property). None changes what the user sees; record (a) and (c) as `Different` in PARITY. | PARITY |

#### Roster

| WU | Unit | Rust | C# lines | Test kind |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| W8-18l | kinds, predicates, message table, colours, scale, custom-draw decision, attach / detach / subclass procedures | `dialogs::native::painter::{mod, geometry}` | 1-333 plus the pure helpers of 335-612 | G (golden `painter-kinds`, 503 rows) + N |
| W8-18m | double buffering, drawing helpers, push button, list view row correction | `painter::controls` | 335-657 | N + pure `push_button_draw` |
| W8-18n | check box, radio, combo, progress, trackbar, group box, etched line, header filler, flat borders | `painter::{widgets, frame}` | 659-990 | N + pure layouts |
| W8-18o | Win32 port, router, recovery read | `dialogs::secondary_window_port` | 21-373 and 380-445 of `Win32SecondaryWindowPort.cs` | F (golden `secondary-window-supports`, 44 rows) + N |

#### Decisions

| # | Decision | Where |
|---|---|---|
| D-W8B5-401 | **The painter is split by C# method, and the plan's `title_bar` file does not exist.** The plan lists `painter::geometry`, `painter::controls`, `painter::{title_bar, frame}` for three line ranges. The C# painter has no title bar code (the dark title bar is `DarkDialogChrome` + `ChromeOs::apply_title_bar`, W8-18d / W8-13k). The files are `geometry` (18l), `controls` (18m), `widgets` and `frame` (both 18n), plus `mod.rs` (18l, the attach / detach / subclass procedures and the `ChromePainter` implementation). The table in the module doc of `painter/mod.rs` maps every C# method to its file. Line numbers of the plan are only approximate (the contracts use method boundaries, as D-W8B5-302). | 18l..18n |
| D-W8B5-402 | **Pure decisions are plain functions with plain inputs, in the file of the unit that draws.** Classification, predicates, the message table, scale, colour keys and the custom-draw decision are in `geometry.rs` (18l) because the procedures need them; the layout of each widget (`push_button_draw`, `check_layout`, `combo_layout`, `trackbar_layout`, `tick_xs`, `progress_fill_width`, `border_thickness`, `border_layers`, `group_box_layout`, `etched_shape`, `header_filler_rects`) is pure and sits with its GDI function in 18m / 18n, returning data (rectangles, points, palette KEYS, flags), so the colour and geometry rules are tested without a window and the GDI function only decodes the control and replays the data. The worked examples in the contracts are hand-computed from the C# formulas (the C# methods are private and need a window, so the oracle cannot call them). | 18l..18n |
| D-W8B5-403 | **`PaintState` is a boxed struct whose address is the `ref_data` and the window property.** `Box::into_raw` in `attach`, `Box::from_raw` at `WM_NCDESTROY` only; `hot` is a `Cell<bool>` (the procedure is re-entered by the `SendMessage` calls of the drawing code); the C# fields `Tracking` and `Reserved` were never read and are not ported. | 18l |
| D-W8B5-404 | **`NativeDialogChromePainter` is a unit struct implementing `chrome::ChromePainter`; there is no global instance.** `production_painter() -> Rc<dyn ChromePainter>` is what `DarkDialogChrome::new` receives (D-W8B5-202). The window property names carry the Rust prefix (`PhotoReviewRs.ChromePaint`, `PhotoReviewRs.ChromeDialog`; D-W8B5-206); the subclass ids `CHMP` and `CHMD` are unchanged. | 18l |
| D-W8B5-405 | **Painter colours: `geometry::color(key)` returns magenta `0x00FF00FF` for an unknown key.** The C# `DialogColors.FromKey` throws `KeyNotFoundException`, which the procedure swallowed (the control lost its dark look). Rust has no swallow (R0003) and the keys are constants: `keys::*` names the 17 keys the painter uses, `keys::ALL` is checked against `pr_app::overlay::palette::TABLE` by a test, and a missing key shows a visible magenta instead of an invisible failure. | 18l..18n |
| D-W8B5-406 | **The list view row correction works on the typed `windows` `NMLVCUSTOMDRAW`** (`&mut NMLVCUSTOMDRAW`, fields `nmcd.dwDrawStage`, `nmcd.dwItemSpec`, `nmcd.uItemState`, `nmcd.hdr.hwndFrom`, `clrText`, `clrTextBk`), never byte offsets (the C# used +24, +56, +64, +80, +84; D-W8B5-212 asserts the layout). The decision is `geometry::adjust_custom_draw(stage, result, selected: impl FnOnce() -> bool)` (the closure runs only in the item pre-paint branch); `controls::adjust_list_view_custom_draw` is the memory part. | 18l, 18m |
| D-W8B5-407 | **Robustness rules of the widget arithmetic.** `Math.Clamp(value, min, max)` throws in C# when `min > max`; Rust `Ord::clamp` panics: use `max(min).min(max)` (the thumb centre of the trackbar, the progress fraction). `Math.Round` of a `double` is `round_ties_even`. The pointer answered by `TBM_GETPTICS` belongs to the control: copy `count - 2` values at once, a null pointer is `None`, an `inner` table that is too short gives `min`. C# unchecked `int` arithmetic on control values (`position - low`, `value - min`) uses `wrapping_sub`. | 18n |
| D-W8B5-408 | **Every GDI object is owned by a guard.** Pens, brushes, the memory DC, the bitmap, the old objects selected into a DC and the font selected into the DC are released / restored on every path, also when a drawing function panics (the C# `try/finally`). Native tests count `GetGuiResources(GR_GDIOBJECTS)` over 50 paints. | 18m, 18n |
| D-W8B5-409 | **Oracle command `painter-kinds`** (`tools/oracle-cs/Commands/W8-18l.cs`, inputs `tests/golden-inputs/painter-kinds.json`, 503 cases): reflection over the internal `NativeDialogChromePainter` (public/internal/private statics `KindFor`, `IsFullPaint`, `HasFlatBorder`, `TracksHover`, `RepaintsAfterSystemPaint`, `S`, `AdjustListViewCustomDraw`, the private consts), `DialogColors`, `CommonControlsInit` of the `PhotoReview` assembly; `kind_for` rows (73) create a real hidden `STATIC` popup and a real child window of the class, so that case kind needs a desktop session. `custom_draw` rows (20) use `hwndFrom = 0`, so the "selected" branch is a hand-written pure test. | D-W8B5-410 | **Panic policy of the painter procedures, and the default chrome wiring.** (a) `control_proc` and `dialog_proc` run their body in `pending_panic::guarded` (D-W8B5-207); on a captured panic the message STILL goes to `def_subclass_proc` (the C# `catch (Exception) {}` then `DefSubclassProc`) and the owner of the native call resumes the panic. This is the opposite of the menu painter (D-W8B4I-9 swallows and logs); the lead may flip one of the two for consistency. Port order 18l, 18m, 18n, each its own PR. | 18l..18n |

The painter units port only the tests that use plain controls. | 18k, 18p..18v, 19 |
| D-W8B5-413 | **The `IServiceProvider` of `Win32SecondaryWindowPort` becomes the trait `SecondaryWindowDialogs`.** Rust has no container (plan section 09). The trait has one method per window (`settings`, `recovery`, `benchmark`, `action_profiles`, `diagnostics`, `skipped_files`, `batch_review`, `custom_zoom`); each builds the dialog from its own unit's constructor and returns a `PreparedDialog<T> { dialog: NativeDialog, finish: Box<dyn FnOnce(i32) -> T> }` (the dialog and the reading of its result: the C# `dialog.Saved`, `dialog.Value`, `dialog.Actions`) or `None` (the C# `GetService<T>() is null` returns, an unreadable journal). The port keeps what is routing and bookkeeping: `supports`, the disposed rule, the open list, the nested modal run with `ready` and the shell loop, the single-instance Benchmark, `dispose` in reverse order. `BenchmarkDialogSeams`, the chrome, the warning sink, `RecoveryDialogSeams` and the settings options are arguments of the production factory, not of the port. The nested Action profiles editor of the Settings window is an `ActionProfilesOpener` holding a `Weak` of the port. | 18o |

The C# let the exception reach the caller's guard (`Different` row, as D-W8B5-2). The C# `VerifyUiThread` is unrepresentable: the port holds `Rc` and is `!Send` (compile-time test, D-W8B3H-4). | 18o |
| D-W8B5-415 | **`ExternalEditors` and `RenamePrompt` are not supported by the Rust port (baseline parity).** The baseline `Supports` lists seven kinds plus Settings; the two extra enum variants of `SecondaryWindowKind` (D-W8B5-4) answer `false`, and the router (which answers `supports = true` for everything) sends them to the cancelled answer. The golden `kinds` row lists the 8 baseline names; the 2 Rust-only variants follow them in the enum. | 18o |
| D-W8B5-416 | **Oracle command `secondary-window-supports`** (`tools/oracle-cs/Commands/W8-18o.cs`, inputs `tests/golden-inputs/secondary-window-supports.json`, 44 cases): reflection over the internal `Win32SecondaryWindowPort` (built with an empty `ServiceCollection().BuildServiceProvider()` and `settingsEnabled` null / `() => true` / `() => false`) and `SecondaryWindowRouter` (null fallback); only `Supports`, `OpenCount`, `IsBenchmarkOpen`, `SettingsEnabled` are read; no window, no dialog. | D-W8B5-417 | **Open dialogs are tracked by a counter id, not by handle or reference.** `open: RefCell<Vec<OpenDialog { id, dialog }>>` and the Benchmark slot use the id (a closed dialog has `hwnd == 0`; `NativeDialog` has no `ptr_eq`); the `closed` callback of the modeless Benchmark holds the id and an `Rc<PortState>` and clears the slot only if it still holds that id. `dispose` clones the handles first and closes them last to first; no `RefCell` borrow is held across `show_modal`, `show_modeless`, `close`, `activate` or a factory call. | 18o |

(c) **Unplanned unit W8-18w**: the production implementation of `SecondaryWindowDialogs` (about 250 lines, Sonnet, N; depends on 18p..18v and 19j..19m; builds each dialog from its constructor with the services of the composition root, includes the RV-A14 journal read with `try_read_recovery_entries`, the warning sink, the clipboard copy, `BenchmarkDialogSeams`). Until it exists, 17c can pass a factory answering `None` for every window. The lead decides whether it is its own row or part of W8-17c. (d) The four post-baseline C# dialogs named in `contracts/README.md` (section "Wave 8 batch B5, group G1") stay unplanned. (e) Reconcile the numbering: 18l, 18m, 18n each get a PARITY row `X-chrome-painter-*` (new). | plan |
| D-W8B5-419 | **`PaintState` counts its live instances (test hook).** The box freed at `WM_NCDESTROY` is a heap allocation, invisible to `GetGuiResources`, so the native leak test of 18l could not detect a leak or a double free. `PaintState` is `#[non_exhaustive]`, built by `PaintState::new(kind)` (counts up), `Drop` counts down, and `painter::live_state_count()` (`#[doc(hidden)]`) reads the `AtomicUsize`. | 18l |
| D-W8B5-420 | **`controls::frame_rects` is `pub`.** The pure test of the four rectangles of `Frame` lives in `tests/` (an integration test crate) and cannot reach a `pub(super)` helper; the helper is a plain `pub fn` returning `[Rect; 4]`, which `frame` uses. | 18m |

(b) The C# tests that drive `ShowRecovery` / `Show*` through the real services (`RecoveryDialogDataSafetyTests.Port_*`, `RecoveryDialogGapTests.Port_*`, `Port_Copy_*`) need the production `SecondaryWindowDialogs`, which is the unplanned unit W8-18w (D-W8B5-418 c); until the lead creates that row they have no owner. The Benchmark `Port_*` tests (3) and `SettingsPortTests` (7) are listed in `W8-18o.md` and ported there with test dialogs. | 18o |

### Wave 8 batch B5, group G5 (W8-18p, W8-18q, W8-18r, W8-18s: ZoomPromptDialog, DiagnosticsDialog, ActionProfilesDialog, BenchmarkDialog): lead decisions

Written by the contract writer of group G5; the lead merges these lines into `contracts/README.md` ("Lead decisions (Wave 8 batch B5)") and may overrule. Where a question had a recommended default it was taken (standing instruction). Numbers `D-W8B5-501..520` are the block reserved for G5, so they cannot collide with G1 (1..11), G2 (201..213), G3 (301..320), G4 (401..418), G6 (601..616), G7 (701..715) or G8 (801..815). Detail and reasons are in `contracts/W8-18p.md` .. `W8-18s.md`.


#### Roster

| WU | Unit | Rust | C# source | Porter | Test kind / golden |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| W8-18p | `ZoomPromptDialog` (+ `DialogStyles`), `DiagnosticsDialog` | `dialogs::{zoom_prompt, diagnostics}` | `Native/ZoomPromptDialog.cs` 134, `Native/DiagnosticsDialog.cs` 205 | Haiku (tests: Sonnet) | F+N; oracle `dialog-zoom-diagnostics` (33 cases) |
| W8-18q | `ActionProfilesDialog` (+ `IDialogWarningSink`, `TaskDialogWarningSink`, `FileFilterText`) | `dialogs::action_profiles` | `Native/ActionProfilesDialog.cs` 414 | Sonnet | F+N; oracle `dialog-action-profiles` (51 cases) |
| W8-18r | `BenchmarkDialog` part 1: ids, items, state, grid sort, language, ALL layout | `dialogs::benchmark::{layout, state}` | `Native/BenchmarkDialog.cs` lines 21-330 and 566-660 | Sonnet | F (+ N for init / layout / language); oracle `dialog-benchmark-layout` (45 cases) |
| W8-18s | `BenchmarkDialog` part 2: commands, run driver, `IBenchmarkView`, grid colours, profile list text | `dialogs::benchmark::{run, results}` | `Native/BenchmarkDialog.cs` lines 332-564 | Sonnet | F+N; oracle `dialog-benchmark-results` (30 cases) |

#### Decisions

| # | Decision | Where |
|---|---|---|
| D-W8B5-501 | **The layout vocabulary of the four dialogs lives in `zoom_prompt.rs`.** `trait DialogMeasure` (the C# `protected` helpers the layout calls: `dx`, `dy`, `line_height`, `measure_item_height`, `measure_text_width`, `measure_text_height`, `item_text`, `button_width`, `button_row_width`; implemented for `NativeDialog` by one-line delegations that are real code in the stub), `Placement` (a recorded `Place`) and `ButtonRowPlan` (a recorded `PlaceButtonRow`). Every dialog's layout is a PURE function `(&dyn DialogMeasure, client size) -> plan` (`layout_plan`, `preferred_client_size`, `minimum_client_size`, `column_widths`); the `DialogContent` hook only applies the plan. So the arithmetic is tested without a window, against (a) a golden produced by the C# class on a window-less instance (preferred / minimum size with every measured height 0) and (b) hand-computed worked examples in the contracts. The types are plain data in the stub, so 18q..18s do not wait for the 18p port. Other B5 groups may reuse them but are not required to. | 18p..18s |
| D-W8B5-502 | **`BenchmarkDialog.cs` is cut at members, not at the plan's line 330.** The plan's "layout, state" (1-330) and "run, results" (330-661) do not match the code: the whole layout (566-660) is in the second half and the grid sort in the first. 18r = ids, items, constructor, `OnInitialized`, columns, `OnClosed`, language change, `SyncRows`, `CompareRows`, `SortBy` and ALL layout (`layout.rs`, `state.rs`); 18s = `OnNotify`, `RowColors`, `ListItemText`, `StaticTextColor`, `OnCommand`, `OnCancel`, `Browse`, `Run`, `Observe`, the `IBenchmarkView` members and the run driver (`results.rs`, `run.rs`). The plan's module names are kept. `benchmark/mod.rs` is final (four `pub mod` lines and the re-exports; lead-owned). `state.rs` holds the one `impl DialogContent for BenchmarkContent`; its hooks call inherent `pub(super)` methods that `run.rs` / `results.rs` add (`command`, `cancel_requested`, `drain_inbox`, `notify`, `list_text`, `static_color`), so no unit edits another unit's file; `BenchmarkContent` declares ALL fields (precedent D-W8B4M-1 / D-W8B5-302) and each file owns a small state struct (`LayoutState`, `RunState`). | 18r, 18s |
| D-W8B5-503 | **`IDialogWarningSink`, `TaskDialogWarningSink` and `FileFilterText` live in `dialogs::action_profiles`** (they are declared in `ActionProfilesDialog.cs`, 18q). Rust: `trait DialogWarningSink { show_warning(owner, title, message) }`, unit struct `TaskDialogWarningSink` with `instance() -> Rc<dyn DialogWarningSink>` (= `NativeTaskDialogPort::new().show(.., TaskDialogKind::Error)`), unit struct `FileFilterText` with `parse(&str) -> Vec<FileDialogFilter>`. Consumers: W8-18o / the production `SecondaryWindowDialogs`, W8-19f (`FilesSettingsPage`, whose draft kept a private twin `parse_filter_text`, D-W8B5-811: switch to this one), W8-19j..19m (`SettingsDialog`: `Options.Warnings`, import / export filters), and the unplanned external-editors dialogs. Lead action: tell the 19f / 19j porters; the stub is already in the tree. | 18q, 19f, 19j..19m |
| D-W8B5-504 | **Borrow discipline of the dialog contents (D-W8B4I-1 applied).** Presenters live in a `RefCell` (`ActionProfilesContent`, `ZoomPromptContent`) or `Rc<RefCell<..>>` (`BenchmarkContent`, shared with the tooltip provider). Every method READS the controls first, then borrows mutably for exactly ONE presenter call, drops the borrow, then WRITES controls / opens dialogs. A modal warning, a file dialog or a nested `ZoomPromptDialog` never runs under a borrow. The `BenchmarkView` the presenter writes to (`run::DialogView`) applies status / progress / running to the controls at once and only RECORDS `refresh_rows` (`rows_dirty`); the grid is rebuilt by `flush_view_effects` after the presenter call, because the rebuild reads the presenter. Read-only callbacks that can fire during a presenter call (row colours, profile text, tooltips) use `try_borrow` and fall back to a neutral answer. `try_borrow_mut` failures are logged and ignored (unreachable on the UI thread; R0003 forbids a panic). | 18p..18s |
| D-W8B5-505 | **The Benchmark run driver replaces the C# `async` presenter.** `pr_app::presenters::benchmark` (W8-10i) is a step machine of `Send` jobs. The dialog hands each job to an injected `BackgroundRunner` (`pr_app::coordinators::zoom_detail`, default `ThreadRunner`, thread names `benchmark-scan` / `-profile` / `-save`); the job's outcome and every progress message are sent to a `CompletionInbox<BenchmarkEvent>` whose wake function posts the private window message `WM_BENCHMARK_WAKE = WM_APP + 0x42` to the dialog window (the handle is an `Arc<AtomicIsize>`, `0` before `WM_INITDIALOG` and after the close); the dialog procedure drains the inbox in arrival order (`on_window_message`) and feeds the events to the presenter (`apply_event`), dispatching each returned step. The language handler (any thread) posts `WM_BENCHMARK_LANGUAGE = WM_APP + 0x43` the same way (the C# `_ui.Post(OnLanguageChanged)`). The C# constructor argument `IUiScheduler ui` is therefore dropped, `LastRunTask` has no twin (tests wait with a message-driven `wait_until` / `flush`), and `Observe` (the faulted-task log) has no twin (jobs catch their panics, W8-10i). Same pattern as the Recovery dialog (D-W8B5-602). The ids do not collide with `WM_APP + 1` (the dispatcher's wake message, a thread message) or the other dialogs' private messages. | 18r, 18s |
| D-W8B5-506 | **Closing the Benchmark window in the middle of a run abandons the run state (a deliberate difference).** C#: `Dispose` cancels the token; the run ends through the normal cancel path and `presenter.IsRunning` becomes false (the test asserts it). Rust: `on_closed` cancels the token (`presenter.close()`); the job still ends through its cancel path and disposes its session ON THE WORKER; its late event finds no window to wake (handle `0`) and is dropped, so the presenter (owned by the dead dialog) never learns. Observable: no Fail row, nothing applied after the close, the session disposed, the token cancelled, the window destroyed, no hang: all asserted. Not observable: `presenter.is_running()` after the close. Record as a `Different` row (W-06). | 18r, 18s |
| D-W8B5-507 | **Optional constructor arguments become `BenchmarkOptions`** (`pick_folder`, `open_in_explorer`, `scan`, `save`, `chrome`, plus the worker seam `runner` and the `pictures_folder` override), `#[derive(Default)]` = every C# `null`. `BenchmarkDialog::new(initial_folder, session_factory, options)`. The `BenchmarkDialogSeams` record of `Win32SecondaryWindowPort.cs` (W8-18o / the production factory) maps its five fields onto it. The same shape is used by the other three dialogs with plain `Option` parameters (`files`, `warnings`, `chrome`, `copy`), because their C# constructors have at most four. | 18r, 18o |
| D-W8B5-508 | **Texts: the ambient language, items frozen at construction.** UI text comes from `pr_l10n::tr::*` (= the C# `Tr`, ambient `Localizer::current()`), read AT CALL TIME for `title()` and for every caption set after creation; the `Items` list is built ONCE in the constructor (the C# list initialiser / `BuildItems()` ran there), so a language change after construction does not change an open dialog's template captions. The Benchmark window is the one exception that re-applies every caption on a language change (`on_language_changed`, as the C#). Presenter calls that need texts get a `FieldContext` from `with_field_context` (D-W8B5-710 pattern, a private twin in `benchmark/state.rs` so 18r does not depend on 19a). No Vietnamese or English literal outside tests. Tests that read or set a language hold the process-wide `serial()` lock of the shared fixture. | 18p..18s |
| D-W8B5-509 | **Culture-sensitive grid sort.** C# `CultureInfo.CurrentCulture.CompareInfo.Compare(a, b, None)` becomes `CompareStringEx(LOCALE_NAME_USER_DEFAULT, 0, ..)` (`state::culture_compare`), falling back to `pr_core::text::cmp_ignore_case` then ordinal when the call fails. .NET 10 on Windows may use ICU, whose tailorings differ from NLS for rare strings; both agree for the first-letter-different ASCII words of the tests and for Latin text in general. `Different` note in PARITY. Numeric columns use the .NET `double.CompareTo` rule (`compare_f64_dotnet`: NaN first). | 18r |
| D-W8B5-510 | **Test kinds and shared fixtures.** Pure tests (golden + fake-measure worked examples) are not ignored; every test that creates a window is `#[ignore = "native: needs desktop"]` and follows D-W8B5-315 (own STA thread, hidden `STATIC` owner, the user simulated inside `ready`, no sleeps). New shared fixtures: `tests/common/dialogs_secondary_fixture.rs` (created by the 18p test porter: `serial()`, `LanguageGuard::{english, vietnamese}`, `EnUs` numbers, `FakeMeasure`), `tests/common/dialogs_benchmark_fixture.rs` (18r: `Rig`, `pump_until`, `select_only`), `tests/common/dialogs_benchmark_run_fixture.rs` (18s: blocking `FakeSession`, `run_two_profiles`, notification builders). Names are unique to the group (AGENTS.md). The Benchmark native tests run on the shell loop of `tests/common/ui_thread.rs` (13c) with the dialog created inside `UiThread::invoke`. | 18p..18s |
| D-W8B5-511 | **Four oracle commands, all reflection over the internal classes (no change to the C# repo, no csproj change).** `dialog-zoom-diagnostics` (`W8-18p.cs`, 33 cases), `dialog-action-profiles` (`W8-18q.cs`, 51), `dialog-benchmark-layout` (`W8-18r.cs`, 45), `dialog-benchmark-results` (`W8-18s.cs`, 30). They pin: the item lists (class, id, text, style, ex style), titles and the `NativeDialog` overrides, label / value / copy texts, `FileFilterText.Parse`, `RowText`, `CompareRows` (27 pairs), the sort-click state, the row colours (COLORREFs) and profile list text, the static colours, the palette keys, the ids, and the preferred / minimum client size of a WINDOW-LESS instance (`RuntimeHelpers.GetUninitializedObject` + `_avgCharWidth`, `_lineHeight`, `_childHosts`, the technique of D-W8B5-313: every measured text height is 0 and `ButtonWidth` is `Dx(50)`). NOT in the oracle: the heights of wrapped text, the `Place` calls, anything with a window; those are the hand-computed worked examples. Lead actions: add to `Program.cs` the four lines `// Commands\W8-18p` `"dialog-zoom-diagnostics" => Commands.W818pZoomDiagnosticsCases(inputs),` / `// Commands\W8-18q` `"dialog-action-profiles" => Commands.W818qActionProfilesCases(inputs),` / `// Commands\W8-18r` `"dialog-benchmark-layout" => Commands.W818rBenchmarkLayoutCases(inputs),` / `// Commands\W8-18s` `"dialog-benchmark-results" => Commands.W818sBenchmarkResultsCases(inputs),`; run the oracle (the references to `PhotoReview`, `PhotoReview.App.Shared`, `PhotoReview.Benchmarking`, `PhotoReview.Core` already exist); add the four README rows given in the contracts. If a reflection member name is wrong the oracle throws with the member name: fix the .cs file, not the contract. | 18p..18s |

No `windows` feature is missing (`Win32_Globalization` for `CompareStringEx`, `Win32_UI_Shell` for `SHGetKnownFolderPath`, `Win32_System_Com` for `CoTaskMemFree` are enabled). | 18r, 18s |

They are `NativeDialog` subclasses that use `IDialogWarningSink`, `FileFilterText` and `ListDialog` (D-W8B5-503 makes the first two importable). `DialogStyles` (inside `ZoomPromptDialog.cs`) is covered by D-W8B5-203.
