### Wave 8 batch B5, group G6 (W8-18t, W8-18u, W8-18v: the Recovery dialog): lead decisions

Written by the contract writer of group G6; the lead merges these lines into `contracts/README.md` ("Lead decisions (Wave 8 batch B5)") and may overrule. Where a question had a recommended default it was taken (standing instruction). Numbers `D-W8B5-601..616` are the block reserved for G6, so they cannot collide with G1 (1..11), G2 (201..213) or G3 (301..320). Detail and reasons are in `contracts/W8-18t.md`, `W8-18u.md`, `W8-18v.md`.


#### Roster

| WU | Unit | Rust (`dialogs::recovery::`) | C# members (method boundaries) | Test kind |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| W8-18t | shell, template, sizes, filter and list | `mod`, `layout`, `list` | 1-233, 235-381, 861-923 | F (oracle `recovery-dialog-layout`, 17 cases) + N |
| W8-18u | details pane, path buttons, buttons and commands, background check, completion pump | `path_panel`, `actions` | 383-666, 792-859, 925-985 | F (oracle `recovery-dialog-details`, 58 cases) + N |
| W8-18v | Retry (Restore), Clear (Dismiss), busy state | `restore`, `dismiss` | 668-788 | F+N (oracle `recovery-dialog-dismiss`, 12 cases; data-safety tests on a real journal) |

#### Decisions

| # | Decision | Where |
|---|---|---|
| D-W8B5-601 | **Shape and slicing.** `RecoveryDialog : NativeDialog, IRecoveryCheckView` is `struct RecoveryDialog { native: NativeDialog, content: Rc<RecoveryContent> }` (D-W8B5-301); `RecoveryContent` implements `DialogContent` and holds ALL state in four small state structs declared by the slice that uses them (`ListState`, `DetailsState`, `ActionsState`, `RestoreState`). The plan's line ranges (330 / 660) cut the file in the middle of the layout and of the check; the slices follow the MODULE names the plan gives and the C# METHOD boundaries (as D-W8B5-302): 18t = fields, constructor, template, sizes, `LayoutControls`, filter and list; 18u = details, path buttons, buttons, commands, background check, message-box helpers, completion pump, `LayoutDetails`; 18v = `Dismiss`, `RetryAsync`, `SetBusy`. `recovery/mod.rs` (18t's) declares the six submodules and is final; the 18u/18v stubs compile against 18t's types, so the three slices are ported in dependency order t, u, v (one PR family, three commits suggested) and each is verifiable alone. | 18t, 18u, 18v |
| D-W8B5-602 | **`async` becomes worker job + inbox + window message + pump.** `RunChecksAsync` and `ExecuteRetryAsync` are already split by the presenter (D-W8-2) into `begin_*`, a `Send` job and `on_check_message` / `finish_retry`. The dialog runs the job with `BackgroundRunner::run` (`ThreadRunner` in production; the tests inject an inline or queued runner) and the job `send`s a `RecoveryCompletion` into a `CompletionInbox`; the inbox wake function calls `PostMessage(dialog hwnd, WM_RECOVERY_COMPLETIONS = WM_APP + 1)` on any thread (the hwnd is an `Arc<AtomicIsize>`, `0` before `on_initialized` and after `on_closed`); `DialogContent::on_window_message` answers it with `pump`. `pump` moves the drained completions to a FIFO `ready` queue and pops ONE at a time with no borrow held, so a nested loop (a message box opened by a handler) that dispatches the wake message again continues the same queue (order kept, nothing handled twice). No `UiScheduler` handle is needed by the dialog. C# `LastCheckTask` / `LastRetryTask` become the counters `check_runs()` / `retry_runs()` plus `pump_completions()` for tests; `Observe` (log a faulted task) is dropped: the presenter logs its own failures and `ThreadRunner` logs a spawn failure. The Benchmark stubs of G5 (`benchmark/run.rs`: `CompletionInbox<BenchmarkEvent>`, `post_wake`, `WM_BENCHMARK_WAKE`) use the same pattern; only the enum and the message constant differ. | 18t, 18u, 18v |
| D-W8B5-603 | **The presenter is a `RefCell` and its callbacks only record.** `RecoveryWindowPresenter` calls its view (`RecoveryCheckView`) and the `set_busy` / `rows_changed` closures from inside `&mut self`. A borrow is held for ONE presenter call and never across an OS call, a hook, `confirm`, `notify`, `place`, `set_item_text`, `enable`: a nested `SendMessage` re-enters the dialog procedure, whose hooks read the presenter. The view is `CheckEffects` (a recorder of `CheckEffect::{RowChanged, CheckStatus, RecheckEnabled, Completed}`); `set_busy` / `rows_changed` push into local vectors; the effects are applied after the borrow ended, in the recorded order (same order as the C#, where the events fired inline). Hooks that must read the presenter use `try_borrow` and answer their default on failure (R0003: a hook never panics). The C# subscription to `ResultChanged` per row disappears (the presenter's `row_changed` replaces it, D-W8-1). | 18t, 18u, 18v |
| D-W8B5-604 | **Rows are indices, the visible list is rebuilt in the same turn, selection is carried by entry id.** C# `_visible` is a list of row objects; Rust keeps `visible: Vec<usize>` AND `visible_ids: Vec<String>` (the entry ids at the same moment) because a row removal shifts the presenter indices while the list still shows the old rows, and `refresh_entries` must read the selected ids from the snapshot, not through the stale indices (otherwise Clear selected would re-select the neighbours of the removed rows); Rust's `visible` is `Vec<usize>` (indices into `presenter.rows()`), valid until the next mutation of the rows (dismiss, a superseded retry), after which `refresh_entries` runs in the same UI turn; every access is `.get(i)` (never indexing). `refresh_entries` remembers the selected entry IDS (not indices) and re-selects those still shown (`carried_selection`), keeps the top index (`top_index_to_restore`), and moves the focus from a hidden list to the filter. `dismiss` takes presenter indices (10g ignores an invalid one). | 18t, 18u, 18v |
| D-W8B5-605 | **Seam types.** `RecoveryDialogSeams { messages: Option<Rc<dyn TaskDialogPort>>, copy: Option<Rc<dyn Fn(&str) -> bool>>, explorer: Option<Rc<dyn Fn(&str) -> Result<(), String>>>, auto_check: bool }` with `Default` = all `None`, `auto_check = true`. The Explorer seam's `Err(String)` stands for the C# `Win32Exception` / `InvalidOperationException` (the only two the C# catches); it is shown with `RECOVERY_EXPLORER_FAILED_TITLE`. The default `start_explorer` is `Command::new("explorer.exe").raw_arg(arguments).spawn()` (no `unsafe`; `raw_arg` keeps the quotes of `/select,"path"` verbatim; the child is not waited for). | 18t, 18u |
| D-W8B5-606 | **Texts come from a `RecoveryTextSource`, not an ambient `Tr`.** `Arc<Localizer>` + `Rc<dyn RecoveryTimeFormatter>` + the `N0` group separator, built by the composition root (17e / 18o), owned by the dialog; `texts()` gives the `RecoveryTexts` the presenter functions take. A language switch is a new dialog (the C# dialog reads `Tr` at creation and on each refresh; the next window opens in the new language, same visible result because the dialog is modal). | 18t, 18u, 18v |
| D-W8B5-607 | **Layout arithmetic is pure over a narrow trait.** `RecoveryLayoutBase` (11 members, the base-class helpers the C# calls: `Dx`, `Dy`, `LineHeight`, `MeasureItemHeight`, `MeasureTextWidth`, `MeasureTextHeight`, `GetItemText`, `Place`, `PlaceButtonRow`, `ButtonRowWidth`, `ButtonWidth`, `SetListItemHeight`) with a thin `impl for NativeDialog` (precedent `ListDialogBase`, D-W8B5-208/316). `layout_controls`, `layout_details`, `place_path_group`, the sizes are free functions over `&dyn RecoveryLayoutBase`, tested with a recording fake and a hand table (contracts 18t and 18u) and, for the sizes, the golden. Arithmetic follows D-W8B5-312 (`wrapping_*`, truncating `/`); the path box height uses `min(max(..))`, not `i32::clamp` (which panics when `min > max`, R0003). | 18t, 18u |
| D-W8B5-608 | **Oracle commands.** `recovery-dialog-layout` (`W8-18t.cs`: constants, `BuildItems`, `ToColorRef`, sizes for 10 metric pairs), `recovery-dialog-details` (`W8-18u.cs`: `BuildDetailsText` via Activator-built internal records, `TextColorOf`, the Explorer argument string on a temp root with `$ROOT` / `$NODRIVE` tokens, `Copy`), `recovery-dialog-dismiss` (`W8-18v.cs`: the Clear flow on a window-less dialog with the message boxes behind a `DispatchProxy` over the internal `ITaskDialogPort`). All use `RuntimeHelpers.GetUninitializedObject(RecoveryDialog)` and reflection (precedent W8-18i), no change to the C# repo. The dispatch lines are in the header comment of each file. The `DispatchProxy` over an internal interface is the one untested assumption (fallback in the header of `W8-18v.cs`: an `IgnoresAccessChecksTo("PhotoReview")` attribute in the oracle project). The `LayoutControls` placements cannot be golden (no window, `Place` is a no-op): hand table. | 18t, 18u, 18v |
| D-W8B5-609 | **Window-less pure tests.** `NativeDialog::new(content)` creates no handle (D-W8B5-304 stub doc), so `RecoveryContent` + `NativeDialog::new` with an inline runner tests the check flow, the pump, `command_action` and the `Hwnd == 0` branch of `dismiss` without a desktop. Hooks and helpers that need the list window check `dialog.item(LIST_ID) == 0` first, exactly like the C#. | 18u, 18v |
| D-W8B5-610 | **C# tests of the file that are not ported here.** `RecoveryDialogDataSafetyTests` `Port_*` tests (`ShowRecovery`, `Dispose`, `Copy`, `Port_Retry_*`, `Port_Clear_*`, `Port_RetryQuestion_*`) and `ReadingTheJournal_ReportsIoAndAccessErrors_ThroughTheCallback` drive `Win32SecondaryWindowPort` and belong to W8-18o (where the production `RetryFn` and `DismissFn` closures are built). The other 11 data-safety tests are 18v's native tests on a real `OperationJournal` + `RecoveryRetryService` on a temp root. `RecoveryRowDrawingTests` (3, offscreen DC) are 18t's (they test the row hooks through the base class `draw_owner_item`, 18k). | 18o, 18v |
| D-W8B5-611 | **One fixture for the three slices.** `crates/pr-platform-win/tests/common/dialogs_recovery_fixture.rs` is written by the 18t test porter (rig, `FakeMessages`, `InlineRunner`, `QueuedRunner`, fixed time formatter, `open`, `pick`, `filter`, `click`, `check_done`) on top of the 18h fixture (`on_sta`, the kit helpers); 18u and 18v only read it and keep extra helpers in their own test files. | 18t, 18u, 18v |
| D-W8B5-612 | **Retry has no golden.** `RetryAsync` starts from the selected list row, which needs the list window; the confirm / delegate / notice order is hand-written from `RecoveryDialogTests` / `GapTests` and pinned by recorder-based native tests. The Clear flow IS goldenable up to the presenter call (window-less) and is. | 18v |
| D-W8B5-613 | **Late results.** A retry that finishes after the window closed is never applied to a view: the worker job checks the shared `closed` flag before AND after its `send` (drain-and-log when it was set in between) and logs (`AppLog::info("Recovery retry finished after the window closed: ..")`, or `error` for an `Err`), and `on_closed` drains the inbox and logs any leftover retry completion; check completions of a closed window are dropped (their token is cancelled by `cancel_check`). While the window is still open but not `Alive` at a given step (closed during a message box) the C# `Alive` checks are kept one for one. | 18u, 18v |
| D-W8B5-614 | **Busy state and cursor.** `restore.busy` mirrors the presenter's `is_retrying` through `apply_busy`, called AFTER the presenter call returned (never inside its `set_busy` callback). `apply_busy` updates the buttons and sets the system arrow / wait cursor at once; the wait cursor over the whole dialog while busy is the base class's `WM_SETCURSOR` answer (`wait_cursor()`, 18k). `can_close_by_user` and `on_cancel` refuse closing while busy; `close()` from outside always closes. | 18t, 18v |
| D-W8B5-615 | **The retry job is never cancelled.** The dialog passes no cancel token (the C# dialog has none); the production closure of 18o uses `CancelToken::new()` and `confirmed_finish_cancelled = true` (the dialog's own pointed question is the confirmation, as in C# `ShowRecovery`). Closing the shell mid-retry closes the window at once; the file work finishes on its thread (INV-5: a half-finished move is worse than a late one) and its result is logged. | 18v |
| D-W8B5-616 | **INV-5 audit rule.** Nothing under `dialogs/recovery` writes, moves or deletes a file: the verifier runs `rg "std::fs::(write|remove|rename|copy|create)|OpenOptions" crates/pr-platform-win/src/dialogs/recovery` (must be empty; `std::fs::metadata` for the Explorer argument is the only `std::fs` use, in `path_panel.rs`) and the data-safety tests compare the file tree (names, sizes, mtimes) and the journal text before and after every declined or Clear action. | 18u, 18v |

#### Notes for the lead (not decisions)

- **Plan edges to add** (code edges): 18t also needs 18b (`TaskDialogPort`), 18d (`DialogItem`, chrome), 18f (`ComboBoxControl`, `ListBoxControl`), 11b, `pr_app::coordinators::zoom_detail::{BackgroundRunner, CompletionInbox}`; 18u needs 18b and 11b; 18v needs 18b, 11b and (tests) `pr_core::file_actions::{journal, recovery_retry}`. The plan lists only 18k / 10g / 18t / 18u. W8-18o additionally needs `RecoveryDialog::new`, `RecoveryDialogServices` and the `Port_*` tests of D-W8B5-610.
- **Oracle wiring**: add the three dispatch lines (header comments) to `tools/oracle-cs/Program.cs` after `// Commands\W8-18i`; no `.csproj` change (PhotoReview.dll, PhotoReview.Core.dll and PhotoReview.App.Shared.dll are already referenced). Run order independent. Golden outputs go to `tests/golden/recovery-dialog-{layout,details,dismiss}.json`. `W8-18v.cs` declares one public top-level class `W818vMessageProxy` (a `DispatchProxy`), the only type outside `Commands`.
- **PARITY rows to add** (`Todo (contract W8-18t/18u/18v)`): `X-recovery-dialog-layout`, `X-recovery-dialog-list`, `X-recovery-dialog-details`, `X-recovery-dialog-actions`, `X-recovery-dialog-restore`, `X-recovery-dialog-dismiss` (INV-5). Recommended `Different` notes at merge: D-W8B5-602 (no tasks: worker job + inbox + wake message), D-W8B5-605 (`Command` + `raw_arg` instead of `Process.Start`), D-W8B5-613.
- **Cross-group**: the pump (D-W8B5-602) is the first dialog-level worker-to-UI mechanism of B5; the Benchmark dialog (G5, 18r/18s) has the same need (progress messages from the engine). G5's `benchmark/run.rs` (seen at the time of writing) uses the same mechanism, so nothing to reconcile; a shared `post_wake` helper could be factored out after both ports merge. The `WM_APP + 1` number is per window class and cannot collide with another dialog.
- **Unplanned C# in the dialog tree** (no row of mine covers them): at the baseline there is none besides `SecondaryWindowFallback.cs` (retired by the plan). The checkout (post-baseline) additionally has `ExternalEditorsDialog.cs`, `ExternalEditorProductName.cs`, `RenamePromptDialog.cs`, `TextPromptDialog.cs` (as G3 noted).
- **Depends on the not-yet-ported base**: `NativeDialog` stubs (18h..18k) compile today; `RecoveryLayoutBase for NativeDialog` is written against their signatures (`dx`, `dy`, `line_height`, `measure_*`, `place*`, `button_*`, `set_list_item_height`, `get_item_text`, `body_font`); if 18i changes one of them the compiler points at `layout.rs`.

### Wave 8 batch B5, group G7 (settings framework: 19a page view, catalog and context; 19b layout elements; 19c page builder): lead decisions

Written by the contract writer of group G7; the lead merges these lines into `contracts/README.md` ("Lead decisions (Wave 8 batch B5)") and may overrule. Where a question had a recommended default it was taken (standing instruction). Numbers `D-W8B5-701..715` are the block reserved for G7, so they cannot collide with the other B5 groups (G1 uses 1..11, G2 201..213). Detail and reasons are in `contracts/W8-19a.md` .. `W8-19c.md`.
Verified while writing: the stubs under `crates/pr-platform-win/src/dialogs/settings/{page_view,catalog,context}.rs` and `settings/builder/*` pass `cargo check -p pr-platform-win --all-targets --locked` and `cargo clippy -p pr-platform-win --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings` and `rustfmt --edition 2024 --check`. The three oracle files (`tools/oracle-cs/Commands/W8-19a.cs`, `W8-19b.cs`, `W8-19c.cs`) were compiled alone against the BCL in a scratch project (they use only reflection, no PhotoReview type) and the two reflection techniques they rely on (a `DispatchProxy` implementing an `internal` interface of another assembly; an `Action<InternalType>` built with `System.Linq.Expressions`) were run in a scratch project on .NET 10. The oracle was NOT run against the baseline and no golden exists yet.

| # | Decision | Where |
|---|---|---|
| D-W8B5-701 | **Page contract shape.** C# `ISettingsPageView` + abstract `SettingsPageBase` become ONE trait `SettingsPageView` whose provided methods are the base-class defaults, plus a helper `PageContextSlot` for the C# `protected Context`. Pages are `Box<dyn SettingsPageView>`; every method that can change page state takes `&mut self`; `Key` is `&str`; a text provider (`Func<string>`) is `TextFn = Rc<dyn Fn() -> String>` (UI thread only, so `Rc`), with the helpers `text_fn` and `fixed_text`. `notification` of `on_command` is `u32`, control ids are `i32` everywhere (`DialogItem.id` is `i32`). | 19a, all pages |
| D-W8B5-702 | **`SettingsPageBuilder.cs` is cut at class boundaries, not at the plan's line 322.** Line 322 is inside `SettingsKeyGridElement`. 19b = lines 1-307 (the `SettingsRow`/`SettingsSliderRow` records, `ISettingsLayoutSurface`, the base class and eight elements) in `builder::rows` (surface, base trait, row, slider, text, buttons) and `builder::groups` (group, check, radio, check flow); 19c = lines 309-645 in `builder::layout` (key cell records and `SettingsKeyGridElement`) and `builder::inputs` (`SettingsPageBuilder`, `ClearGlyph`). `builder/mod.rs` is final (lead-owned, four `pub mod` lines). Rust names drop the `Settings` prefix inside `settings::builder` (`RowElement`, `GroupElement`, ...) except the records the pages use (`SettingsRow`, `SettingsSliderRow`, `SettingsKeyCell`, `SettingsKeyCellSpec`, `SettingsPageBuilder`). | 19b, 19c |
| D-W8B5-703 | **Layout arithmetic never panics.** The C# `int` wraps and `Math.Clamp` throws when `min > max`; Rust uses saturating `i32` operations, `max` then `min` instead of `i32::clamp`, and treats a zero divisor (`dx` returning 0) as "one column" in the key grid. `TextWidth` in the oracle fake is the UTF-16 length times a constant, so the Rust fake in the tests counts `encode_utf16()`, not `chars()`. | 19b, 19c |
| D-W8B5-704 | **The builder's `throw`s are RECORDED errors.** `InvalidOperationException` (the 1000th control of a page) and the two `ArgumentException`s of `radio` become `SettingsBuilderError::{TooManyControls, RadioNeedsTwoOptions, RadioHintCountMismatch}`; the builder stores every violation (`errors()`, `error()` = first), the failing call returns zero ids (a `radio` returns `texts.len()` zeros) and leaves the state the C# call had left when it threw (the counter is incremented even by the throwing `Add`, items added before the throw stay, no layout element is added, a rejected `radio` changes nothing and keeps a pending group cut). The frame (19j) refuses to build a page whose builder has an error and shows the pending placeholder instead. The C# `ArgumentNullException` cases are unrepresentable. | 19c, 19j |
| D-W8B5-705 | **A page that has no context yet gets a DETACHED one.** C# `Context` is `null!` until `Attach`; Rust `PageContextSlot::context()` returns `SettingsPageContext::detached()` (reads answer empty/false/0/-1, writes do nothing) so a page never panics or branches on `Option`. | 19a, pages |
| D-W8B5-706 | **Ownership of pages, presenter and borrows.** The dialog keeps pages as `Rc<RefCell<dyn SettingsPageView>>` (or an equivalent) and the presenter as `SharedSettingsPresenter = Rc<RefCell<SettingsPresenter>>`. While a page runs (`on_command`, `load`, ...), the host reaches OTHER pages with `try_borrow_mut` and skips the one that is already borrowed (the C# `SetActionsJson(json, source)` reloads every page except the source). Nobody holds a `RefCell` borrow (of a page or of the presenter) across a modal call: file dialog, warning, action profiles editor (the same rule as T5 of 18a). | 19a, 19j..19m, pages |
| D-W8B5-707 | **`SettingsDialogOptions` is not visible to pages.** `SettingsPageContext` is a facade over the seam trait `SettingsPageHost` (all methods `&self`); what pages read from the C# options record is a method of the host: `files()`, `version()`, `open_url(url)` (`Options.OpenUrl ?? DefaultOpenUrl`), `start_explorer(args)` (`Options.StartExplorer ?? SettingsPresenter.TryStartExplorer`), `show_action_profiles(owner, actions)` (`Options.ShowActionProfiles`, with `Err(ActionProfilesUnavailable)` for "not wired or threw"), `decoder_factory()`. The record itself, `Warnings`, `Target`, `Pages`, `PhysicalMemoryBytes`, `ControlKeyDown` belong to the frame (19j) which implements `SettingsPageHost`. `Context.Options` is not ported (its two uses become `open_url`). | 19a, 19g, 19j |
| D-W8B5-708 | **Catalog table and testable forms.** `catalog::FACTORIES: &[(&str, PageFactory)]` (`PageFactory = fn() -> Box<dyn SettingsPageView>`) lists the seven pages in ALPHABETICAL key order (the C# `Dictionary` initializer order, which `implemented_keys` enumerates). The stub ships it empty because the page stubs belong to the 19d..19i contract group; the 19a porter fills the seven lines as `("Diagnostics", || Box::new(DiagnosticsSettingsPage::new()))` ... using the page types `pages::{diagnostics, display, files, general, mouse, performance, shortcuts}::{Diagnostics,Display,Files,General,Mouse,Performance,Shortcuts}SettingsPage` with `pub fn new() -> Self` (the 19d..19i contracts must expose exactly these names; the lead checks when merging the contract PR). `create_pages_with` and `pending_keys_with` take the table and the navigation keys so the tests use fakes; the test of the real `create_pages()` is `#[ignore = "waits for W8-19d..19i"]` until the last page constructor is ported (page constructors are `todo!()` before that, a function pointer to them is fine). | 19a, 19d..19i |
| D-W8B5-709 | **`PostToUi` becomes `ContextUiAction` + `SettingsUiPost`.** C# `PostToUi(Action)` is "safe from a background thread" (the update-check answer). A Rust page holds `Rc` state and cannot move itself to a worker, so the action is `Box<dyn FnOnce(&SettingsPageContext) + Send>` (it gets the context of the window it runs in and reaches the presenter and the controls through it), posted through `SettingsPageContext::post_to_ui` or, from a worker, through the `Send + Sync` handle `SettingsPageContext::ui_post()`. Posting to a closed window is a silent no-op (INV-9 spirit: the answer after Cancel is dropped). The host implements it with a private window message and an `Arc<Mutex<VecDeque<ContextUiAction>>>` (19j). | 19a, 19g, 19j |
| D-W8B5-710 | **Presenter calls that need texts use the ambient language.** C# reads the ambient `Tr`; the Rust presenter takes a `FieldContext`. `context::with_field_context(|ctx| ...)` builds it from `Localizer::current()` and `pr_l10n::localizer::number_culture()` (D-W8-4) so a page writes `with_field_context(|ctx| presenter.borrow_mut().open_update_page(&mut open, ctx))`; `page_view::page_title(key)` is the same for `SettingsPresenter::page_title`. | 19a, pages |
| D-W8B5-711 | **Three oracle commands and what each golden pins.** `settings-catalog` (`W8-19a.cs`: `ImplementedKeys` order, `PendingKeys`, `IsComplete`, `CreatePages` keys and type names, `PageTitle` for 7 keys and 3 bad keys, `PendingSettingsPage`), `settings-layout` (`W8-19b.cs`: the eight layout elements and the key grid against a deterministic fake `ISettingsLayoutSurface`, 75 cases; the goldens carry `surface_def`, so they are self-contained), `settings-builder` (`W8-19c.cs`: 29 op-script cases pinning items, styles, texts, registrations, the element tree and the thrown exceptions). The interface is implemented with `DispatchProxy` and the `Group` callback with `Expression.Lambda`; both techniques need no change to the C# repo. Lead wiring: three dispatch lines in `Program.cs` (the header of each file has its line). | 19a, 19b, 19c |
| D-W8B5-712 | **One layout fixture for 19b and 19c.** `crates/pr-platform-win/tests/common/settings_layout_fixture.rs` (created by 19b) holds the golden-driven fake surface and the element builder from a case's `element` spec; 19c adds `tests/common/settings_builder_fixture.rs` (the op-script interpreter). Both names are unique to the group (AGENTS.md). `tests/common/settings_host_fixture.rs` (created by 19a: `RecordingHost`, a recording `SettingsPageHost`) is the shared fake for the page units 19d..19i and the frame units; its API is binding (see `contracts/W8-19a.md`). | 19a..19c, 19d..19m |
| D-W8B5-713 | **Group lookup for the frame.** The C# frame builds `_groupsById` by `Flatten`ing the builder's elements and flips `group.Visible`. Rust gives the builder `flatten()`, `set_group_visible(title_id, visible) -> bool`, `group_visible(title_id) -> Option<bool>` and `elements_mut()`; the trait `SettingsLayoutElement` has `as_group()` / `as_group_mut()` (the C# `is SettingsGroupElement`). 19j should use them instead of a second tree walk. | 19b, 19c, 19j |
| D-W8B5-714 | **Ids and the page stride are the frame's.** `SettingsDialog.PageIdBase(i) = 1000 * (i + 1)` and `PageIdStride` are 19j's (they are `SettingsDialog` members). The builder only takes `id_base`; its limit is 999 controls (`MAX_CONTROLS`). | 19c, 19j |
| D-W8B5-715 | **Oracle limits.** The Win32 side of the builder (`Button` text of a real window, the `&` display, `WS_GROUP` arrow-key behaviour of the dialog manager) is not in the oracle; one native test creates every declared item as a real control and compares class, style bits and text (19c), and the arrow-key behaviour of two independent radio groups (`TwoRadioGroups_OnOnePage_AreIndependent_AndSetRadioIndexCheckExactlyOne`) is deferred to the frame tests of 19j/19k, which own a dialog. | 19c, 19j |

### Wave 8 batch B5, group G8 (settings pages: 19d Diagnostics and Performance, 19e Display, 19f Files, 19g General, 19h Mouse, 19i Shortcuts): lead decisions

Written by the contract writer of group G8; the lead merges these lines into `contracts/README.md` ("Lead decisions (Wave 8 batch B5)") and may overrule. Where a question had a recommended default it was taken (standing instruction). Numbers `D-W8B5-801..815` are the block reserved for G8, so they cannot collide with G1 (1..11), G2 (201..213), G3 (301..320) or G7 (701..715). Detail and reasons are in `contracts/W8-19d.md` .. `W8-19i.md`.
Verified while writing: the stubs under `crates/pr-platform-win/src/dialogs/settings/pages/` pass `cargo check -p pr-platform-win --all-targets --locked` (with the other B5 writers' files as they were at the time). The six oracle files (`tools/oracle-cs/Commands/W8-19d.cs` .. `W8-19i.cs`) were NOT compiled or run (the lead does; they reuse `W819cDump`/`W819cType` of `W8-19c.cs` through the shared `partial class Commands`, so `W8-19c.cs` must be present). No golden exists yet. All 238 `Tr.*` names used by the seven C# pages were mapped by script to the generated `pr_l10n::tr::*` functions (all exist, all take no argument).

| # | Decision | Where |
|---|---|---|
| D-W8B5-801 | **Shape of a page.** One struct per page named as in C# (`DiagnosticsSettingsPage` ... `ShortcutsSettingsPage`, the names D-W8B5-708 promised), `pub fn new() -> Self` (+ `Default`), a `PageContextSlot`, the control ids as private `i32` fields, and the C# test accessors as `pub fn ids() -> <Page>Ids` (a `Copy` struct whose fields are the C# tuple names in snake_case) plus the list accessors (`exif_checks`, `title_checks`, `journal_radios`, `menu_checks`, `cell`, `marked_properties`, `languages`). `new()` is `todo!()` in the stubs, so the ignored `real_create_pages_returns_the_seven_pages_in_nav_order` of 19a stays ignored until the last page lands. `pages/mod.rs` (seven `pub mod`, alphabetical) is final and lead-owned; `settings/mod.rs` got the one line `pub mod pages;`. | 19d..19i |
| D-W8B5-802 | **Texts.** C# `() => Tr.SomeName` is `text_fn(pr_l10n::tr::some_name)` (the generated function is a plain `fn() -> String`): the Rust name is the C# name in snake_case (a `_` before every capital, digits stay attached: `Tr.SettingsFitWidthAnchor2Label` is `tr::settings_fit_width_anchor2_label`). The presenter statics the C# calls inside a provider (`PreloadWindowHint`, `ToolbarOpacityHint`, `KeyboardZoomStepPercentHint`, `ContextMenuItemLabel`) become `text_fn(|| with_field_context(|c| SettingsPresenter::...(c)))` (D-W8B5-710). The two non-language literals `"100%"` and `"200%"` of the General view mode combo and the marker `"\u{26A0} "` stay literals (they are symbols; D-W8-4 is about language text). A text read once (`Tr.X` outside a provider, for example in `update_turbo_note`) is `tr::x()` at call time. | 19d..19i |
| D-W8B5-803 | **Declaration pattern.** `declare(&mut self, page)` calls `page.group(title, \|g\| { ... })`; the closure must not borrow `self`, so it assigns the ids it gets to local `let mut` variables and the method copies them into the fields after the group returns. A builder method that returns a list is read with `.first().copied().unwrap_or(0)` (never `[0]`: R0003). When the builder recorded an error (`SettingsPageBuilder::errors()` non-empty) the page does nothing special: the frame (19j) refuses the page. | 19d..19i |
| D-W8B5-804 | **Six oracle commands, shared driver.** `settings-pages-diag-perf` (W8-19d), `settings-page-display`, `settings-page-files`, `settings-page-general`, `settings-page-mouse`, `settings-page-shortcuts` (W8-19e..19i). Every command has a `declare` case: the oracle creates the real C# page, runs `Declare` on a `SettingsPageBuilder(id_base)` and dumps items, texts, notes, key boxes, warnings, combo items, sliders and the element tree (the `W819cDump` of 19c) plus the control ids read from the private fields. `id_base` is `SettingsDialog.PageIdBase(index)` = 1000 x (position in the navigation order + 1): General 1000, Display 2000, Mouse 3000, Performance 4000, Shortcuts 5000, Files 6000, Diagnostics 7000. The shared driver (`W819pDeclare`, `W819pPageType`, `W819pSnake`) lives in `W8-19d.cs`; the other five files call it. Lead wiring: six dispatch lines in `Program.cs` (the header of each file has its line). The oracle must be built and run against the C# BASELINE `d92d6aae` (D-W8B5-808). Load/Collect/commands need a window in C# and are tested in Rust against `PageHost` (D-W8B5-807), not by golden. | 19d..19i |
| D-W8B5-805 | **A page never relies on the echo of its own writes.** C# `SetWindowText` on an edit box makes the OS send `EN_CHANGE` to the dialog synchronously, and the C# Clear button of the Shortcuts page relies on it ("EN_CHANGE of the box refreshes the warning"). While a page method runs the Rust frame holds the page borrowed (D-W8B5-706), so the echo would be dropped (`try_borrow_mut` fails). Rule: after a page writes a control whose `EN_CHANGE`/`CBN_SELCHANGE`/`BN_CLICKED` the C# code relied on, the page calls the handler's work itself (Shortcuts: `refresh()` after the Clear button empties a box). It is harmless if the frame also delivers the notification later. `Load` runs with the frame's `_loading` flag set (notifications swallowed), exactly as in C#. | 19i (and the frame, 19j) |
| D-W8B5-806 | **`ActionsForEditor` texts.** `SettingsPresenter::actions_for_editor(json, &dyn SettingsTexts)` needs the localized default action names, the presenter keeps its `settings_texts` private and the host trait has no accessor. Recommended default: the Shortcuts page passes `pr_core::settings::app_settings::EnglishSettingsTexts` (the English `Action {n}` / `Backup` only matter when the JSON has a nameless action or is the literal `null`; recorded `Partial` in PARITY). Lead action (one line, `pr-app`): add `pub fn settings_texts(&self) -> Arc<dyn SettingsTexts>` to `SettingsPresenter` (or give `SettingsPageContext` a `settings_texts()`), then switch the page. | 19i |
| D-W8B5-807 | **One stateful fake host for the page tests.** `tests/common/settings_page_fixture.rs` (created by 19d; binding API in `contracts/W8-19d.md`) holds `PageHost`, a `SettingsPageHost` that STORES what the page writes (text, check, combo, slider with the trackbar clamp of `TBM_SETRANGE`/`TBM_SETPOS`, enabled, visible, tooltip, focus), records one call line per write, queues `ui_post` actions in an `mpsc` channel (run on the test thread by `run_posted`/`wait_posted`) and offers `presenter(configure)` / `FakeShell`. 19a's `RecordingHost` stores nothing (reads are presets), so it cannot test "write then read back" pages (Performance range, Mouse summary, Shortcuts refresh). The plan has no edge from 19e..19i to 19d for this file: lead action, merge 19d first (soft edge, test files only) or let the 19e..19i test porters wait for the fixture. | 19d..19i |
| D-W8B5-808 | **Post-baseline C# is not ported.** The C# HEAD (33b02fbe) changed three pages after the baseline `d92d6aae`: Files (+69 lines: "Manage editors...", the "send RAW" check, the editors summary line, `Context.EditExternalEditors`), Shortcuts (two more optional Action rows `OpenInDefaultEditor` and `RenameFile`) and Mouse (`ContextMenuRows(includeShellOnly: true)`), plus `SettingsPageContext` (+24 lines). They belong to the external editors sync patch (P-17, W2-S1: `ExternalEditor`, `AppSettings`, `ShortcutMappings`, `SettingsFormState` first). The Rust presenter and form state are at the baseline, so the pages are too; the goldens must be produced from a C# worktree at `d92d6aae`. Unplanned C# files (new in HEAD, not in the plan, NOT owned by these units): `Dialogs/Native/{ExternalEditorsDialog, ExternalEditorProductName, RenamePromptDialog, TextPromptDialog}`. | 19f, 19h, 19i |
| D-W8B5-809 | **The update check of the General page.** C# `RunUpdateCheckAsync` (`async`, `await CheckForUpdateAsync`, `PostToUi`) becomes: UI thread `begin_update_check` -> a `BackgroundRunner` (`Rc<dyn Fn(Box<dyn FnOnce() + Send>) -> bool>`; production = a named `std::thread`, tests = inline) runs `job.run()` -> `SettingsPageContext::ui_post().post(action)` with a `ContextUiAction` that, back on the UI thread, calls `finish_update_check(completed, ctx)` and updates the controls through the `&SettingsPageContext` it receives (D-W8B5-709). The page text `_updateStatusText` is an `Arc<Mutex<String>>` shared with the action (locked only for a copy or an assignment, poison ignored). `UpdateCheckJob` and `UpdateCheckCompleted` are proven `Send` by a compile-time assertion in the stub. A spawn failure (`false`) ends the check with `tr::update_status_failed_bad_response()` and re-enables the button. The C# `UpdateCheckTask` test accessor has no twin (the tests drive the runner and the host queue). | 19g |
| D-W8B5-810 | **Key capture is not in the Shortcuts page.** The plan row 19i says "(key capture)" and test kind F+N. In C# the capture is `SettingsDialog.HookKeyBox/KeyBoxProc/CaptureKey/IsKeyBox` (the key box subclass, `ImmAssociateContext`, `WM_KEYDOWN` -> `KeyIdMapping` -> `SettingsPresenter.CaptureShortcut`) and belongs to the frame units 19j/19k (the stubs of 19c already declare the key boxes and `capture_shortcut` is merged in 10e). The page itself is pure, so 19i has no native test; the native half of the plan row moves to the tests of 19j/19k (`APressedKey_IsWrittenAsItsCanonicalName_ThroughTheBoxSubclass` and the 10 key capture tests of `SettingsShortcutsPageTests`). Lead action: re-label 19i as F in the plan and add the key capture tests to the 19k contract. | 19i, 19j, 19k |
| D-W8B5-811 | **`FileFilterText.Parse`** (the `Name\|pattern\|Name\|pattern` string of `tr::dialog_file_filter_executable`) is declared in `ActionProfilesDialog.cs` = W8-18q, whose stub already has `dialogs::action_profiles::FileFilterText::parse` (D-W8B5-503 of G5 says 19f switches to it). 19f therefore writes NO private twin: `browse_for_editor` calls `FileFilterText::parse`; the parser cases are 18q's tests. Code edge 19f -> 18q (not in the plan; D-W8B5-515 already lists it). | 19f |
| D-W8B5-812 | **`InitialFolder` parity details.** `File.Exists(path)` is true only for an existing FILE (a directory, a missing path, an invalid path and any I/O error give false); `Path.GetDirectoryName` of an existing file gives its directory with `/` normalised to `\`, `""` for a bare relative name (`"x.exe"`) and `null` for a root. Rust: `std::fs::metadata(path).is_ok_and(\|m\| m.is_file())` then `Path::parent()` mapped to `String` with `/` replaced by `\`; `Some("")` stays `Some("")` (T8), a root stays `None`. `string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace` is `path.trim().is_empty()`. | 19f |
| D-W8B5-813 | **Dependencies the plan does not list (lead action).** Every page unit also needs 19a (`SettingsPageView`, `PageContextSlot`, `SettingsPageContext`, `TextFn`, `with_field_context`), 19b/19c (builder, already the plan edge), `pr_app::presenters::{settings, settings_form_state, settings_field_map}` (10b..10e), `pr_l10n::tr`. 19f also needs 18a (`ShellFileDialog`, `FileDialogFilter`) and `pr_platform_win::wic::availability` (W4-11b, merged) and `pr_imaging::webp_heic::WicCodecSupport`; 19g needs `pr_app::localization::language_options` (04a, merged); 19h needs `pr_core::settings::context_menu`; 19i needs `pr_app::services::shortcut_key_name` (04c, merged) and `pr_core::settings::shortcuts` (plan edges 05c/10e). The seven `FACTORIES` lines of 19a need the seven `new()` of this group: nothing in this group edits `catalog.rs`. | all |
| D-W8B5-814 | **Notification constants.** `notification` is `u32` (D-W8B5-701). `BN_CLICKED` = `crate::win32::dialog_native::BN_CLICKED` (u32), `CBN_SELCHANGE` = `crate::win32::control_constants::CBN_SELCHANGE as u32`, `EN_CHANGE` has no shared constant: `pub const EN_CHANGE: u32 = 0x0300;` in `performance.rs` and `shortcuts.rs` (as the C# pages each define their own `EnChange`). | 19d, 19h, 19i |
| D-W8B5-815 | **Insertion-ordered dictionaries are `Vec`s.** C# `Dictionary<ContextMenuItemId, int> _menuChecks` and `Dictionary<string, SettingsKeyCell> _cells` are enumerated by `Load`, `Collect`, `Attach` and the tests in insertion order (T10); Rust keeps `Vec<(ContextMenuItemId, i32)>` and `Vec<(String, SettingsKeyCell)>` and looks up by linear search (7 to 27 entries). `HashSet<string> _marked` is a `BTreeSet<String>` (the order of its enumeration is not observable except in the order of the label rewrites, which no test may depend on). | 19h, 19i |

### Wave 8 batch B5, group G9 (the Settings window: 19j frame, 19k working state and key capture, 19l commands and messages, 19m layout and close): lead decisions

Written by the contract writer of group G9; the lead merges these lines into `contracts/README.md` ("Lead decisions (Wave 8 batch B5)") and may overrule. Where a question had a recommended default it was taken (standing instruction). Numbers `D-W8B5-901..918` are the block reserved for G9, so they cannot collide with G1 (1..11), G2 (201..213), G3 (301..320), G7 (701..715) or G8 (801..815). Detail and reasons are in `contracts/W8-19j.md` .. `W8-19m.md`.

Verified while writing: the stubs under `crates/pr-platform-win/src/dialogs/settings/dialog/` (15 files) pass `cargo check -p pr-platform-win --all-targets --locked`, `cargo clippy -p pr-platform-win --all-targets --locked -- -D warnings` and `rustfmt --edition 2024 --check` (with the G1..G8 files as they were at the time). The four oracle files (`tools/oracle-cs/Commands/W8-19j.cs` .. `W8-19m.cs`) were NOT compiled or run (the lead does); they are written against the baseline `d92d6aae` sources and reach every `internal` type by reflection. No golden exists yet. `SettingsDialog.cs` is NOT identical at the baseline and in the C# working tree (the checkout is newer; read the baseline with `git -C ../PhotoReview show d92d6aae:<path>`).

#### Roster

| WU | Unit | Rust (`dialogs::settings::dialog::`) | C# lines of `SettingsDialog.cs` (method boundaries) | Test kind (plan -> contract) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| W8-19j | spine: state, options, items, routing, handle, page creation, navigation | `frame`, `create`, `nav` | 1-332, 333-438, 566-631, 778 | N -> F+N (golden `settings-dialog-frame`) |
| W8-19k | working form, control accessors, key capture, page host | `state`, `dirty`, `keybox`, `host` | 464-564, 633-743, 1097-1181, 1418, 1421 (+ Rust-only `host`) | F+N (golden `settings-dialog-state`) |
| W8-19l | commands, save, import/export, forwarding, queue, language | `apply`, `validate`, `messages` | 745-776, 795-907, 909-1095 | F+N (golden `settings-dialog-apply`) |
| W8-19m | frame layout, scrolling, page surface, close | `footer`, `page_layout`, `surface`, `close` | 440-462, 780-793, 1183-1279, 1281-1416, 1424-1512 | N -> F+N (golden `settings-dialog-layout`) |

#### Decisions

| # | Decision | Where |
|---|---|---|
| D-W8B5-901 | **Shape: handle + content + weak page host + one deliberate `Rc` cycle.** The C# `SettingsDialog : NativeDialog` is `SettingsContent` (implements `DialogContent`, holds ALL state in `Cell`/`RefCell`) wrapped by the handle `SettingsDialog { native: NativeDialog, content: Rc<SettingsContent> }` (precedent D-W8B5-301, D-W8B3H-18: a content must not store its `NativeDialog`). Pages reach the window through `SettingsPageContext` over `host::PageHost`, which holds a `Weak<SettingsContent>` (so the content owns the pages and nothing loops); after the window is gone every host call answers the neutral value of the detached context (D-W8B5-705). The one exception to "never store the handle" is the field `link`: every content method takes `&NativeDialog`, hooks pass the one they get, and the weak adapter has no hook to get one from, so the content stores a clone in `link` in `initialize` and TAKES it in `close::on_closed` (the `NativeDialog` -> content -> `link` -> `NativeDialog` cycle exists only between window creation and close; a dialog that is never shown never creates it). If W8-18h later adds `NativeDialog::downgrade()`, `link` becomes a `Weak` with no other change. | 19j..19m |
| D-W8B5-902 | **`SettingsDialog.cs` is cut at METHOD boundaries, not at the plan's lines 378/756/1134.** The plan's three cuts fall inside `EnsurePageCreated` (333-427), inside `Warn`/`ExportSettings` (856-907) and inside the scroll code (1134 is the accessor block); the plan's module names are kept (`frame, nav, state, dirty, apply, validate, footer, close`) and seven modules are added (`create`, `keybox`, `host`, `messages`, `page_layout`, `surface`), see the roster. 19j is the largest (about 520 C# lines: the spine plus page creation and navigation), the others about 300 to 370. `dialog/mod.rs` is final (lead-owned: 14 `pub mod` lines and the re-exports); `settings/mod.rs` got `pub mod dialog;`. Every file is `impl SettingsContent` and the unit that owns a file is the only one that edits it; ALL state structs, the `DialogContent` impl and every forwarding method of the handle are declared in `frame.rs` (precedent D-W8B4M-1), so 19k..19m never edit `frame.rs`. Methods that cross units are in the stubs with final signatures, so each slice compiles alone. | 19j..19m |
| D-W8B5-903 | **Constructor and options.** C# `SettingsDialog(current, store, decoderFactory, localization, updateChecker, options, chrome)` is `SettingsDialog::new(SettingsDialogArgs { current, presenter: SettingsPresenterOptions, decoder_factory, options, chrome })`: the store, localization, update checker, shell integration, executable path and RAM size are parts of `presenter` (they are what the Rust presenter takes). `SettingsDialogOptions` loses `PhysicalMemoryBytes` (it is `presenter.physical_memory_bytes`; two fields could disagree); `Pages` are MOVED in (`Vec<Box<dyn SettingsPageView>>`); `OpenUrl` returns `Result<(), String>` instead of throwing; `ShowActionProfiles` returns `Result<Option<Vec<ReviewAction>>, ActionProfilesUnavailable>`; `Version` has no assembly attribute to default from, so `None` is an empty version text and the composition root (W8-17e) passes `BuildStamp::version()`. `new` returns `Err(SettingsDialogError::PageCountMismatch { expected, actual })` for the C# `ArgumentException`. The `chrome` argument is passed to `NativeDialog::with_chrome`. | 19j |
| D-W8B5-904 | **Page borrow protocol (extends D-W8B5-706).** Pages are `Rc<RefCell<Box<dyn SettingsPageView>>>`. Every frame call into a page is `try_borrow_mut` and a page that is already borrowed (it is the one running, and its own write came back as a notification) is SKIPPED, never panics: `on_command` of a borrowed page answers `true` (swallowed echo, D-W8B5-805), `focus_target` answers `None`, `collect`/`load`/`on_closed`/`on_language_changed` are skipped. Consequence checked against the baseline pages: only the Shortcuts page calls `CollectActionsJson`/`CommitActionsJson` and only the Mouse page calls `CurrentShortcutTexts`; no page's `Collect` writes `ActionsJson` (the Files page explicitly has none), so skipping the caller changes no result. The caller of `set_actions_json` is excluded by key anyway. | 19j..19m |
| D-W8B5-905 | **State in `Cell`/`RefCell`, copy out then act.** No `RefCell` borrow (a page, the presenter, a builder, `placed`, `view.pages`, `link`, the queues) is held across an OS call, a page call, a hook, a callback or a nested modal loop (D-W8B4I-1). The one sanctioned exception is the SHARED borrow of a page's builder during `layout_page`'s element loop (the elements only read the surface; no message handler of a page control needs the builder). `loading` is a `LoadingGuard` (RAII, restores the previous value). | 19j..19m |
| D-W8B5-906 | **Presenter rules.** (a) The presenter (`Rc<RefCell<SettingsPresenter>>`) is borrowed for ONE call at a time; `try_save` returns a value (`SettingsSaveResult`) and the warning/focus work happens after the borrow ended. (b) `import_settings_from(path, refresh_view, ctx)` is a `&mut self` call during which the pages must not read the presenter (their `load` calls `context.presenter().borrow()` and would panic): the frame's `refresh_view` only RECORDS `p.load_fields()` and returns `Ok(())`; after the call the LAST recorded state goes through `load_all` once (the presenter's failure arm refreshes twice, imported then previous; the final screen is the same as the C#). (c) `reset_active_page` needs a throw-away presenter (`new SettingsPresenter(Settings, store: null, localization: null)`), and the Rust presenter cannot hand back its texts, shell integration or RAM size, so `SettingsContent::new` captures a `ScratchSeed` from `args.presenter` before the options move. (d) `Err(Hresult)` of the Open/Save dialogs (the C# threw) is a CANCELLED pick, not a warning and not a panic. | 19j, 19l |
| D-W8B5-907 | **C# state that is never read is not ported.** `_dirty[]` is set in six places (page create, activate, load-all, set-actions-json, relayout, language refresh) and cleared in `LayoutPage`, but nothing reads it: it is dropped, and the module `dirty` of the plan holds the control accessors whose C# bodies end in `RelayoutActivePage`. `PageSurface.Origin` is written by `LayoutPage` and read by nothing; the Rust field is kept for parity. `_created` and `_scroll`/`_contentHeight` are real state (`PageView`). | 19j, 19k, 19m |
| D-W8B5-908 | **Subclass `ref_data` is a registry key, never a pointer.** The C# put a `GCHandle` in `refData`. The native base's cookie (`from_cookie`) is `pub(super)` to `native::dialog`, so the settings code cannot recover the content from it. Instead `SettingsContent::register_subclass_key` stores a `Weak<SettingsContent>` in a `thread_local!` map under a non-zero counter value and that value is the `ref_data` of `forward_proc` and `key_box_proc`; `forget_subclass_key` (in `on_closed`) removes it, after which a late message to a child window finds nothing and goes to `def_subclass_proc`. Nothing is dereferenced from `ref_data`, so a message that arrives after the free (children are destroyed after `on_closed`) is safe by construction. Both procedures run under `pending_panic::guarded` (extends D-W8B5-314). | 19j, 19k, 19l |
| D-W8B5-909 | **Two queues for `PostToUi`.** The cross-thread queue is `WindowPoster` (`Send + Sync`: an `AtomicIsize` hwnd and a `Mutex<VecDeque<ContextUiAction>>`, D-W8B5-709): `post` queues and wakes the window with `WM_APP + 0x36`; a closed window (`hwnd == 0`) drops posts and clears the queue. UI-thread-only work (the one user is the external-editor focus the C# posted from `OnInitialized`) is a `LocalAction` (`Box<dyn FnOnce(&SettingsContent, &NativeDialog)>`, not `Send`) in `local_queue`, woken by the same message; the handler runs the local queue first, then the cross-thread one, and an action that posts more is run in the next wake, not the same drain. | 19j, 19l |
| D-W8B5-910 | **Language subscription.** `Localizer::on_current_changed(closure)` (any-thread callback) is made in `initialize`; the closure holds only an `Arc<WindowPoster>` and posts `WM_APP + 0x35`; the `CurrentChangedSubscription` is dropped in `on_closed`. `refresh_localized_texts` always runs on the UI thread, guarded by `in_language_refresh`. Pages that were not created yet are localized when they are created (their texts and combo items are asked from the providers at creation). | 19j, 19l |
| D-W8B5-911 | **Layout is a pure plan; the surface is thin.** `footer::layout_plan(&dyn DialogMeasure, w, h, page_count) -> SettingsLayoutPlan` is the C# `LayoutControls` as data (placements in the C# call order, the list item height, the viewport size), over the shared `zoom_prompt::{DialogMeasure, Placement}` (D-W8B5-501); `page_layout` holds the scroll arithmetic as pure functions (`clamp_scroll`, `bar_target`, `wheel_target` with banker's rounding, `scroll_bar_info`, `ensure_visible_target`); `surface::PageSurface` keeps the one-`SetWindowPos`-per-control cache and the combobox repaint (`placement_change`, `combo_drop_height` are pure). The cache is written only after a successful `set_window_pos` (the C# ignored the result; D-W8B5-917). | 19m |
| D-W8B5-912 | **Four oracle commands and what each golden pins.** All four build the REAL `SettingsDialog` through its constructor with no window (Hwnd 0, so `Item(id)` is 0 and every OS call is a no-op), the same trick as W8-18r. `settings-dialog-frame` (`W8-19j.cs`, also the SHARED driver of the other three: `W819jNewDialog`, `W819jGet`, `W819jCall`, `W819jSetField`, two `DispatchProxy` recorders for the `internal` interfaces `IDialogWarningSink` and `IShellFileDialog`): the 19 frame items with class/id/text/style/ex_style, ids and constants, `PageIdBase`/`ViewportId`/`PageOf` arithmetic, page keys, five Ctrl+Tab decisions. `settings-dialog-state` (`W8-19k.cs`): the key capture table for virtual keys 0..255 with and without the extended bit (`KeyIdMapping.FromVirtualKey` + `SettingsPresenter.CaptureShortcut`), the `ActionsJson` sequences, the group/plain visibility book-keeping, the key box ids. `settings-dialog-apply` (`W8-19l.cs`): the focus owner (page, control id) of every `SettingsFocusField`, five Save flows (valid, changed, invalid shortcut once and twice, duplicate shortcut) with the recorded warnings, three Export cases and five Import cases against the recording file dialog and warning sink. `settings-dialog-layout` (`W8-19m.cs`): `Margin`/`FieldHeight`/`ButtonHeight`/`ScrollBarWidth`, preferred and minimum sizes, the viewport size after `LayoutControls`, and the scroll offsets after `SetScroll`/`ScrollByWheel`/`ScrollByBar` (private fields set by reflection). Limits, recorded in each file header: placements themselves are not observable without controls (hand-derived Rust tests); the thumb codes see track position 0; a `SaveFailed` outcome needs a store with an `IAppPaths` (hand-written Rust test with a failing store); messages that carry .NET parser or OS text are recorded as `message_nonempty`. Lead wiring: four dispatch lines in `Program.cs` (the header of each file has its line: `"settings-dialog-frame" => Commands.W819jSettingsDialogFrameCases(inputs)`, `"settings-dialog-state" => Commands.W819kSettingsDialogStateCases(inputs)`, `"settings-dialog-apply" => Commands.W819lSettingsDialogApplyCases(inputs)`, `"settings-dialog-layout" => Commands.W819mSettingsDialogLayoutCases(inputs)`); four rows in `tests/golden-inputs/README.md` (`| settings-dialog-frame | W8-19j SettingsDialog | frame items, ids, constants, Ctrl+Tab |` and likewise for state, apply, layout); `W8-10b.cs` (`SettingsFrom`) and `W8-19j.cs` must be present; run the oracle against the C# BASELINE `d92d6aae`. The plan's test kind of 19j and 19m changes from N to F+N (as D-W8B5-810 did for 19i). | 19j..19m |
| D-W8B5-913 | **Native test conventions of the Settings window.** As D-W8B5-315: every OS test is `#[ignore = "native: needs desktop"]`, runs on its own STA thread (`fixture::on_sta`), owns its owner window and simulates the user with `SendMessage`/`IsDialogMessage` inside `ready`, no fixed sleeps. The shared fixture `tests/common/settings_dialog_fixture.rs` is written by the 19j test porter (fake pages, recorders, `show`, `go_to`, `choose`, `tick`, ...; it builds on `tests/common/dialogs_dialog_fixture.rs` of 18h) and only read by 19k..19m. Tests that need the real pages carry `#[ignore = "waits for W8-19d..19i"]` as well; the key capture tests need the real Shortcuts page (19i). The four pixel tests of the C# (`SettingsScrollRepaintFixTests`, `SettingsScrollPaintTests`, `SettingsTabAndEditCenterTests`, `SettingsComboFocusPaintTests`) are ported by 19m into one file `settings_dialog_paint_e2e.rs`, need the production painter of 18l..18n (until then `default_chrome()` paints nothing: D-W8B5-305) and a hidden desktop, and keep zero pixel tolerance. Not ported: `SettingsTimingTests` and `Report_ScrollStepCost_OnTheDisplayPage` (reports without assertions), `SettingsSnapshotTests` (screenshots for a human), `SettingsPortTests` (the router, 18o). The lead runs `--include-ignored` once on an interactive session before any N row of the Settings window becomes Done. | 19j..19m |
| D-W8B5-914 | **Key capture is a pure function plus plumbing.** `keybox::key_box_text(virtual_key, extended)` = `SettingsPresenter::capture_shortcut(key, key.name())` with `key = key_mapping::from_virtual_key(vk, extended)`; `resolve_virtual_key` handles `VK_PROCESSKEY` (229): the real key from `ImmGetVirtualKey` when it is not 0 (on Windows 11 the OS answers 229 itself when nothing is being processed, which stays 229 and is reserved, so nothing is written); `is_swallowed_message` lists the eleven messages the box swallows (`WM_CHAR`, `WM_SYSCHAR`, `WM_DEADCHAR`, `WM_SYSDEADCHAR`, `WM_IME_CHAR`, `WM_IME_STARTCOMPOSITION`, `WM_IME_COMPOSITION`, `WM_PASTE`, `WM_CUT`, `WM_CLEAR`, `WM_UNDO`; `WM_COPY` is allowed). Plain Tab is never captured (`classify_dialog_key`), Ctrl+Tab is a page cycle only for `WM_KEYDOWN` (not `WM_SYSKEYDOWN`). The text write makes the OS send `EN_CHANGE` synchronously; the page sees it as one edit (the page is not borrowed then: the key comes from the loop). | 19j, 19k |
| D-W8B5-915 | **Plan-edge corrections (lead action).** The plan lists 19j: 19c, 18k; 19k: 19j, 10e; 19l: 19k; 19m: 19l, 19d..19i. The pages 19d..19i are NOT a code dependency of any slice (the frame compiles against `SettingsPageView`); they are a dependency of the tests that use the real pages (marked `waits for W8-19d..19i`). | plan |

Each slice still has its own contract, owned files, pure tests and Done-when; the pure/window-less tests of a slice run as soon as its own functions are ported only if they do not touch a stub of a later slice (the contracts say which do). | plan |

#### Notes for the lead (not decisions)

- `crates/pr-platform-win/src/dialogs/settings/mod.rs` got `pub mod dialog;` appended (one line plus a comment); nothing else outside `dialog/` was edited.
- The four oracle files share a driver in `W8-19j.cs` and use the private `SettingsFrom` of `W8-10b.cs` (`static partial class Commands`, same class). If the oracle project has `TreatWarningsAsErrors`, the three `public class W819j*Proxy` types are top-level (a `DispatchProxy` implementation must be public), as `W818vMessageProxy` in `W8-18v.cs`.
- `SettingsPageHost::open_url` default (`ShellExecuteExW` with `SEE_MASK_FLAG_NO_UI`, like .NET's `Process.Start`) is the one new OS call outside the dialog base; it lives in `dialog/host.rs` (`default_open_url`). The General page contract (19g) said the host provides it.
- Native tests of 19j..19m that use the real pages cannot be un-ignored before 19d..19i are merged; the real-page golden checks (`page_types`, `page_item_counts`, `focus_targets`, the 27 key box ids) are `#[ignore = "waits for W8-19d..19i"]` for the same reason.
- The `Settings` window opens through `Win32SecondaryWindowPort.ShowSettings` (18o): its `SecondaryWindowDialogs` adapter builds `SettingsDialogArgs` (store, localization, update checker, shell integration, decoder factory, file dialog, warning sink, `show_action_profiles` closure that opens the Action profiles dialog modal over `owner`) and reads `saved()`/`settings()` after `show_modal` returns; `catalog::is_complete()` decides whether the router offers Settings at all.

### Not resolved (needs a product or lead decision; acted on as recommended, P-45)

- 806: `SettingsPresenter::settings_texts()` (or `SettingsPageContext::settings_texts()`) is missing in `pr-app`; the Shortcuts page uses `EnglishSettingsTexts` for action names until it is added (Partial in PARITY). Needs a small pr-app amendment before 19i is ported.
- 19l `reset_active_page`: the C# scratch presenter uses the default RAM size and shell integration; the contract reuses the real presenter's (deliberate change). Keep the contract (recommended) or copy the C# default.
- 901: the live `NativeDialog` stored in `SettingsContent.link` is a deliberate `Rc` cycle cleared in `on_closed`; a `NativeDialog::downgrade()` in 18h would remove it.
- A failed Open/Save dialog is treated as a cancelled pick (C# throws); a warning could be shown instead.
- 18p/18q/18s: the real-dialog "dark background and readable text" rows of `NativeDialogChromeTests` for the zoom, diagnostics, action-profiles and benchmark dialogs are not ported (18m checks plain child windows only, D-W8B5-201); whether to port them is a product call.
- Panic policy of the painter procedures (capture and resume, D-W8B5-410) differs from the popup menu painter (swallow and log, D-W8B4I-9); one of the two may be flipped for consistency.
- Benchmark window closed mid-run abandons the presenter state instead of the cancel path (`Different`, D-W8B5-506); .NET ICU vs NLS grid sort (D-W8B5-509).
- The native tests of this batch (`#[ignore = "native: needs desktop"]`) need a desktop session and COM; the pixel tests of 19m pass vacuously until 18l..18n are ported.

### Not verified

No port exists: every body is `todo!()`. The oracle was run (twice, identical) but no Rust test compares against the new goldens yet, so the golden-driven tests listed in the contracts stay red until their units are ported. The hand-computed tables in the contracts (W8-18t, 18u, 18m, 18n worked examples) were recomputed by the reviewers, not by the oracle. No native behaviour was run.

## Wave 8 batch B6 (shell start-up and the exe: `pr_app::startup`, `pr_platform_win::shell_startup`, `pr_shell::startup`, units W8-17a..17e, W8-20a, W8-20b)

Contract-only batch: 7 contracts (`contracts/W8-17a.md` .. `W8-17e.md`, `W8-20a.md`, `W8-20b.md`), stubs in the three crates (every body `todo!()`; modules `startup`, `shell_startup`, `startup` plus the exe files `main.rs`, `entry.rs`, `build.rs`, `build_support/res_writer.rs` and the UI smoke test stubs in `crates/pr-shell/tests/`), 7 new oracle commands with inputs in `tests/golden-inputs/` and goldens in `tests/golden/` generated at C# `d92d6aae`. Three contract groups wrote them: H1 17a..17c, H2 17d..17e, H3 20a..20b. The per-group decision tables follow verbatim (D-W8B6-1..19 H1, 20..39 H2, 40..54 H3; numbers are unique), then the lead reconciliation.

| W8-17b | `contracts/W8-17b.md` | F + P | `startup-cloak` (16), `shell-show-command` (8) |
| W8-17c | `contracts/W8-17c.md` | F + P | `shell-composition-registrations` (1 case, 50 descriptors) |
