### Wave 8 batch B6, group H1 (W8-17a, W8-17b, W8-17c): decisions, lead actions and cross-unit notes

Written by the contract writer of group H1. The lead merges the table into `contracts/README.md` ("Lead decisions (Wave 8 batch B6)") and may overrule a row. Numbers `D-W8B6-1..19` are the block reserved for H1. Detail and reasons are in `contracts/W8-17a.md`, `W8-17b.md`, `W8-17c.md`. Where a contract question had a recommended default it was taken (standing instruction, P-45 style).

| # | Decision (acted on) | Unit |
|---|---|---|
| D-W8B6-1 | **Three module homes for the start-up (the plan names one).** `pr_app::startup` = logic with no window and no `windows` crate (`launch`, `forwarded_open`, `error_log`, `context`; 17d/17e append `options`, `instance`, `settings`, `recovery`, `run`, `shutdown` when they name only `pr-app` types); `pr_platform_win::shell_startup` = native parts (`dpi`, `placement`, `cloak`, `warmup`, `locale`); `pr_shell::startup` = everything that names both crates or the shared graph (`composition_root`, `secondary_dialogs`, and the `ShellStartup` run loop of 17d/17e, which needs 09b). Reason: D-W8B1-1 (`pr-platform-win` depends on `pr-app`, `pr-shell` depends on both) and the plan row's `startup::{dpi, launch, ...}` / `shell_startup::{...}` names, which cannot all be true in one crate. The three `mod.rs` indexes are created by the H1 writer; 17d/17e writers append lines (re-read before editing). `pr-shell/src/lib.rs` gets `#[cfg(windows)] pub mod startup;`, `pr-platform-win/src/lib.rs` gets `#[cfg(windows)] pub mod shell_startup;`, `pr-app/src/lib.rs` gets `pub mod startup;`. | 17a, 17b, 17c |
| D-W8B6-2 | **Instance names.** `ShellInstanceNames.Prefix` (`"PhotoReviewShell"`, NE-6, keeps the WPF app and the Win32 shell from forwarding to each other) is `pr_core::identity::INSTANCE_PREFIX` (`"PhotoReviewRs"`, R0002) re-exported as `launch::INSTANCE_NAME_PREFIX`; there is no Rust WPF twin, and the prefix already differs from both C# prefixes. The C# test `InstancePrefix_IsNotThePrefixOfTheWpfApp` becomes "not `PhotoReview`, not `PhotoReviewShell`, equals the identity constant". The env override (`PHOTOREVIEW_SHELL_INSTANCE_PREFIX`) is 17d's and is renamed `PHOTOREVIEW_RS_INSTANCE_PREFIX`. | 17a |
| D-W8B6-3 | **`ShellErrorLog` takes `ErrorDetails`, not an exception.** Rust errors have no type name, HRESULT or stack. `ErrorDetails { type_name, hresult, message, stack: Vec<String>, inner }` is built by `ErrorDetails::from_error` (io errors get `std::io::Error` and the `HRESULT_FROM_WIN32` of `raw_os_error`, everything else `Error` and `E_FAIL`; stack = the `Backtrace` lines at the report call) or by the reporter that knows better. `format` is byte-for-byte the C# text (CRLF, 300-unit message cut, 12 frames, inner line) so the goldens compare exactly. Two mutexes instead of the C# one `Gate` (a path mutex and a file mutex) so `report` never holds the path lock while writing. `Different` row `X-startup-error-log`: type names and stacks are Rust's. A cut that splits a surrogate pair writes U+FFFD (hand-written test, not golden: JSON cannot carry a lone surrogate). | 17a |
| D-W8B6-4 | **Forwarded open: no async, one thread-crossing point.** `ForwardedOpenTarget::open_path` returns when the open has been STARTED (D-W8-2); later failures are the window's to report. The coalescer calls its `OpenCallback` on its worker thread: `ui_open_callback(ui)` posts a `Send` job carrying only `Option<String>`; the job finds the `Rc<ForwardedOpenRouter>` through a `thread_local!` `Weak` set by `attach_to_current_thread` on the UI thread. The router is the C# `ShellStartup.OnForwardedOpen` + its three fields (pending request kept until the window exists, first path wins, replayed once by 17d with `take_pending`). `FORWARD_COALESCE_WINDOW` = 750 ms lives in `forwarded_open.rs`; 17d passes `move |paths| coalescer.submit(paths)` to `InstanceScope`. `IsMinimized` is inside the C# `try`, so the Rust `is_minimized` returns `Result<bool, WindowGone>`. | 17a |
| D-W8B6-5 | **`ShellStartupContext`.** The C# record holds `Task<AppSettings>` and `Task<WindowPlacementData?>`; Rust has `StartupTask<T>` (a named worker thread whose result is taken once) and the existing `PrefetchHandle`; the process start is an `Instant`. The struct is not `Clone`. It is data for feature packages and diagnostics: no unit reads it yet except 17d, which builds it. | 17a |
| D-W8B6-6 | **`StartupCloak` thread model.** All state in `Arc<CloakInner>` with a `Mutex`; the safety deadline runs on a timer thread (`schedule_on_thread`: a `WindowCancel` wait; dropping the handle cancels and wakes it) and only POSTS `inner.reveal()` to the `UiScheduler`; seams are `Send + Sync` closures (`SetCloakedFn`, `RevealedFn`); no seam, callback or timer-handle drop runs under the lock. The gate semantics are the C#'s exactly (only a SUCCESSFUL cloak closes the `Hide` gate; `Reveal` without `Hide` still sets `revealed`). `StartupCloak` implements the interim `host::main_window::core::StartupCloakPort` (D-W8B4M-4, D-W8B4X-1: the consumer 13g was ported first, so the port trait stays; a rename-only follow-up is the lead's). | 17b |
| D-W8B6-7 | **Placement: ports, catch, saver.** `WindowPlacementNative` is a trait with `NativeWindowPlacement` (unit value). `apply_before_show` keeps the C# catch-all as `catch_unwind` (log `"Could not restore window placement before show"`, answer `None`); `save` maps `IOException`/`UnauthorizedAccessException` to every `io::Error` (log `"Could not save window placement"`, answer `false`). `ShellPlacementSaver` (store + native + log) implements the interim `PlacementSavePort` that 13g calls. `show_command_for` returns the `SW_*` integers (3 / 1 / 4). | 17b |
| D-W8B6-8 | **`ShellNoticeSink`, `SecondaryWindowFallback`, `IShellLauncher` and the `UnwiredShellFolderDialog` default are not ported.** The sink's only consumer was the retired fallback (plan "Retired C#", D-W8B5-3): the router answers "cancelled" for a window the port cannot run. 17e drops the `ShellNoticeSink.Attach` line of `CreateMainWindow`. `UnwiredShellFolderDialog` stays in `folder_dialog.rs` for tests; the root always registers `NativeShellFolderDialog` (the `AddShellOverlay` line wins over the unwired default in the C#). `Different` rows `X-startup-composition-root`. | 17c |
| D-W8B6-9 | **User-locale services are part of 17c** (`shell_startup::locale`). `DiagnosticsPresenter::build`, `ListDialogContent::for_batch_review` and `RecoveryTextSource` take `NumberFormatting` / `RecoveryTimeFormatter` / a group separator / a "T" time text that C# got from `CurrentCulture`; the three traits exist in `pr_app` (W8-10) but nothing implements them. `WindowsNumberFormatting`, `WindowsRecoveryTimeFormatter`, `group_separator()` and `local_long_time_text()` use `GetNumberFormatEx`, `GetLocaleInfoEx`, `GetDateFormatEx`, `GetTimeFormatEx` and `SystemTimeToTzSpecificLocalTime`. The percent PATTERN per culture is a small documented table (no CLDR in Win32): only en-US is verified against C#, other cultures are a `Different` row (the only consumer is the Diagnostics hit-rate cell). Alternative: a unit W8-17f; the same code. | 17c |
| D-W8B6-10 | **The seam between the composition root and the shared graph (W8-09b) is a plain struct.** `DialogServices` has one field per `GetService<T>()` the C# port made (`settings_store`, `localization`, `update_checker`, `decoder_factory`, `journal`, `recovery_retry`, `file_system`, `metrics`, `explorer_snapshot`, `settings_texts`, `shell_integration`, `physical_memory_bytes`, `runner`, `benchmark_sessions`); 17e fills it from the 09b registry and calls `ShellServices::bind_dialog_services`. A trait over the registry was rejected because 09b is contracted in parallel and its type names are not fixed. `pr-shell` gains the dependency `pr-l10n` (the production factory builds `RecoveryTextSource` from `Arc<Localizer>`): the contract writer already edited `crates/pr-shell/Cargo.toml` and the `pr-shell` entry of `Cargo.lock` (one line each), so `cargo check --locked` passes. | 17c |
| D-W8B6-11 | **Late binding of the graph.** The C# `Lazy<ISecondaryWindowPort?>` is `LazySecondaryPort`; its factory needs the graph, the graph needs the dialog service. `ShellServices` owns a `GraphSlot = Rc<OnceCell<Rc<DialogServices>>>` that the factory reads on the first `get`. `LazySecondaryPort` keeps an absent answer forever, so 17e MUST bind before the first secondary window; the factory logs `"Secondary windows were requested before the service graph was bound"` when it did not. No closure of the dialogs may hold a strong `Rc` of `ShellServices` (cycle). `FolderPicker` is not `Clone`: the dialog service gets its own instance next to the shared one. | 17c |
| D-W8B6-12 | **`DialogServices.file_system` is required** (`Arc<dyn FileSystem>`, not `Option`): the C# `GetService<IFileSystem>()` was always registered in production and the Rust `RecoveryWindowOptions` needs a `RecoveryChecker` (`RecoveryFileCheck::new(file_system)`). The UI scheduler is not an argument of the production factory: the Rust `BenchmarkDialog` has no `ui` parameter (D-W8B5-505). | 17c |
| D-W8B6-13 | **Warm-ups.** `warm_settings_parser` / `warm_graphics` spawn a named thread each and wrap the body in `catch_unwind` (the C# `catch (Exception) when not OOM`); the testable bodies are `warm_settings_parser_now` / `warm_graphics_now`. `NoStripeScheduler` replaces the C# inline `NoDispatcher`: a stripe is never scheduled for the 64x64 warm-up image, and the `StripeScheduler` contract forbids running synchronously. The warm-up renderer uses the culture name `"en-US"` (the Rust constructor needs one; the C# parameterless constructor used the UI culture). | 17c |
| D-W8B6-14 | **Plan-edge corrections (lead action; apply to `docs/WAVE-8-PLAN.md` when the first B6 port starts).** 17a also needs 03a (`PrefetchHandle`), 11b, 11e, 04d, W2-19. 17b also needs 13g (`StartupCloakPort`, `PlacementSavePort`), 11b. 17c also needs 18a, 18b, 18c, 18e, 18h..18k (`ListDialog` as `DialogContent`), 18o, 18p..18v, 19j..19m, 16i, 13c, 13d, 12d, 12f, 12i, 04c, 10f, 10h (09b stays soft). 17d/17e also need 17a..17c in code: `ShellLaunch::parse`, `ForwardedOpenRouter`, `new_forward_coalescer`, `error_log::configure`, `ensure_per_monitor_v2`, `StartupCloak`, `ShellPlacementSaver`, `apply_before_show`, `show_command_for`, `ShellServices::build`/`bind_dialog_services`, `warm_*`. 20a needs 17a (`ensure_per_monitor_v2`). 14f's `MainWindowMenuServices::report_shell_error` is bound to `error_log::report` by 17e (14f open question 3). | plan |
| D-W8B6-15 | **What 17d/17e must do with these pieces** (cross-unit assumptions; each is an open question of the unit that makes it): (a) call `ensure_per_monitor_v2(None)` and `warm_settings_parser` first, `warm_graphics` only when `options.warm_graphics`; (b) `ShellServices::build` once on the UI thread with an owner provider over a `Weak` of the main window (`0` once destroyed); (c) after the 09b graph exists: `bind_dialog_services(Rc::new(DialogServices { .. }))`, then `error_log::configure(Some(config_dir.join(FILE_NAME)))`; (d) `StartupCloak::new(Arc<dyn UiScheduler>, options with on_revealed = start-up mark "windowRevealed")`, `hide(hwnd)` BEFORE the first `ShowWindow`, `apply_before_show` only when not smoke, then `show_command_for`; pass `Rc<StartupCloak>` as `MainWindowDeps.cloak` and `Rc<ShellPlacementSaver>` as `MainWindowDeps.placement`; (e) `ForwardedOpenRouter::new()` + `attach_to_current_thread()` before the instance scope is created, `new_forward_coalescer(ui, log)` and `coalescer.submit` into `InstanceScope::with_options`, `router.set_handler(Rc::new(ForwardedOpenHandler::new(adapter, log)))` after the window exists, then `take_pending` and replay; (f) `ShellStartupContext` built after `ShellLaunch::parse`; (g) before the owner window is destroyed: `secondary_port_host.dispose()` on the UI thread (D-W8B5-11 b); (h) `StartupTask::spawn` for the settings load. | 17d, 17e |
| D-W8B6-16 | **Oracle commands (the writer did NOT run the oracle).** Five commands, three files: `W8-17a.cs` (`shell-launch`: 21 launch cases + constants; `shell-error-log`: 14 format, 3 thrown, 5 truncate, 2 unusable, 3 report cases + constants), `W8-17b.cs` (`startup-cloak`: 15 scripts + constants; `shell-show-command`: 8 rows), `W8-17c.cs` (`shell-composition-registrations`: 1 case listing the baseline `ServiceCollection`). Inputs in `tests/golden-inputs/{shell-launch, shell-error-log, startup-cloak, shell-show-command, shell-composition-registrations}.json`. Lead actions: (1) add to `tools/oracle-cs/Program.cs` inside the `JsonArray cases = command switch { ... }`: `// Commands\W8-17a`, `"shell-launch" => Commands.W817aShellLaunchCases(inputs),`, `"shell-error-log" => Commands.W817aShellErrorLogCases(inputs),`, `// Commands\W8-17b`, `"startup-cloak" => Commands.W817bStartupCloakCases(inputs),`, `"shell-show-command" => Commands.W817bShowCommandCases(inputs),`, `// Commands\W8-17c`, `"shell-composition-registrations" => Commands.W817cRegistrationsCases(inputs),`; (2) add to `OracleCs.csproj` inside the `<ItemGroup Condition="'$(CSharpSrcDir)' != ''">`: `<Compile Include="$(CSharpSrcDir)\src\PhotoReview.App.Shared\LaunchArguments.cs" Link="cs-baseline\LaunchArguments.cs" />`, `<Compile Include="$(CSharpSrcDir)\src\PhotoReview.Shell.Win32\Startup\ShellLaunch.cs" Link="cs-baseline\ShellLaunch.cs" />`, `<Compile Include="$(CSharpSrcDir)\src\PhotoReview.Shell.Win32\Startup\ShellErrorLog.cs" Link="cs-baseline\ShellErrorLog.cs" />` (17b and 17c need no csproj change: reflection into `PhotoReview.dll`); (3) run the oracle for each command; the 17c command creates a `Win32UiDispatcher` (a hidden message window) in the oracle process: if that hangs or fails in the oracle host, the fallback is to drop the `ConfigureServices` call and list the registrations from the source by hand (the Rust mapping test needs only the 12 host service type names and the two feature lines); (4) add rows to `tests/golden-inputs/README.md`: `shell-launch | W8-17a ShellLaunch | Parse over a temp tree: file/folder choice, canonical folders, smoke flag, relative paths, quoted drive root`; `shell-error-log | W8-17a ShellErrorLog | Format text, frame limit, Append truncation, unusable paths, Configure/Report`; `startup-cloak | W8-17b StartupCloak | scripted state machine with an injected DWM seam and a manual timer`; `shell-show-command | W8-17b ShellPlacement.ShowCommandFor | state x activate table`; `shell-composition-registrations | W8-17c ShellCompositionRoot | service registrations of the baseline root`. | tools |
| D-W8B6-17 | **Not covered by an oracle** (internal seams the oracle cannot implement): `DpiAwarenessBootstrap` (`IDpiAwarenessNative`), `ForwardedOpenHandler` (`IForwardedOpenTarget`), `ShellPlacement.ApplyBeforeShow`/`Save` (`IWindowPlacementNative`), `ShellWarmup`, `Win32SecondaryWindowPort` building bodies. Their expectations are hand-written from `DpiAwarenessBootstrapTests`, `ForwardedOpenHandlerTests`, `ShellPlacementTests`, `RecoveryDialogDataSafetyTests`/`RecoveryDialogGapTests`, `ShellWiringTests` (listed per contract). | 17a, 17b, 17c |
| D-W8B6-18 | **PARITY rows to add (the lead owns `docs/PARITY.md`).** `X-startup-launch`, `X-startup-error-log` (**Different**: type/HRESULT/stack come from `ErrorDetails`), `X-startup-dpi` (17a); `X-startup-cloak`, `X-startup-placement` (17b; `Partial` until the native tests ran on a desktop); `X-startup-warmup`, `X-startup-composition-root` (**Different**: `ShellNoticeSink` / fallback retired), `X-dialogs-production-factory`, `X-locale-services` (**Different** for non-en-US percent patterns) (17c). Rows C-18, C-19 -> `Partial (W8-17a)`; C-16, C-13/C-13b wiring -> `Partial (W8-17c)`; `X-dialogs-secondary-port` loses the sentence "production `SecondaryWindowDialogs` is part of W8-17c". | docs |
| D-W8B6-19 | **Native test conventions (this group).** Every test that creates a window, calls the real DWM, creates a GPU device, shows a dialog or changes process-wide state is `#[ignore = "native: needs desktop"]`, runs on its OWN thread (COM tests initialise and uninitialise COM there), joins with a bound and uses `recv_timeout(30 s)` or a posted `WM_CLOSE` loop with a 30 s deadline instead of sleeping or polling; the DPI test and the current-directory test are alone in their test executables; `error_log::configure` tests share one `Mutex` and reset the path in a guard; no test touches the user's real data root, config, Recycle Bin or window placement file (`TempRoot` only). | 17a, 17b, 17c |

#### Open items (recommended defaults taken, listed in the contracts)
- 17a Q4: the instance prefix env override is 17d's; 17b Q1: the interim ports stay (D-W8B4X-1); 17c Q1: `DialogServices` as a struct (D-W8B6-10); 17c Q2: locale services in 17c (D-W8B6-9); 17c Q3: `sink_factory` stays the concrete `Rc<Win32PresentationSinkFactory>` until amendment A11 lands; 17c Q7: the production `BenchmarkSessionFactory` (`production_session_factory(BenchmarkSessionDeps)`) is built by 17e/09b.
- Cross-unit assumptions made without a stub to check: 09b's registry can hand out `Arc<SettingsStore>`, `Arc<OperationJournal>`, `Arc<RecoveryRetryService>`, `Arc<dyn FileSystem>`, `Arc<ReviewMetrics>`, `Arc<LatestExplorerSnapshot>`, `SharedLocalization`, `Arc<dyn UpdateCheck>`, `Arc<dyn ImageDecoderFactory>` (the pointer types are those of the merged pr-core/pr-app items; if 09b chooses another handle type only the field type of `DialogServices` changes); 18h..18k adds `impl DialogContent for ListDialog` (D-W8B5-208) which `skipped_files` / `batch_review` need; 18o's `try_read_recovery_entries` is implemented.
- Not done and out of scope here: the Rust `Program.cs` / `main.rs` (W8-20a), `ShellStartup` (17d/17e), the shared graph (09b), the external-editor and rename dialogs (P-44).

### Wave 8 batch B6, group H2 (W8-17d, W8-17e): decisions, lead actions and cross-unit notes

Written by the contract writer of group H2. The lead merges the table into `contracts/README.md` ("Lead decisions (Wave 8 batch B6)") and may overrule a row. Numbers `D-W8B6-20..39` are the block reserved for H2 (H1 used 1..19). Detail and reasons are in `contracts/W8-17d.md` and `contracts/W8-17e.md`. Where a contract question had a recommended default it was taken (standing instruction, P-45 style).

| # | Decision (acted on) | Unit |
|---|---|---|
| D-W8B6-20 | **The cut of `ShellStartup.cs` is moved to method and block boundaries, and follows the plan's MODULE list, not its line numbers.** The plan's "1 to 343 / 343 to 685" splits the second-instance `if` of `StartupCoreAsync` in two. 17d owns lines 1-82 (options), 253-359 (the first half of `StartupCoreAsync` up to the `instanceLock` mark) and 517-565 (the early-decode plan/prepare/start, one concern used from both halves); 17e owns 84-252 (class, `Run`, `RunLoop`, `FailStartup`), 360-516 (second half, `CreateMainWindow`, smoke, `ReadPlacement`) and 567-685 (recovery, forwarded open glue, shutdown). Module homes follow D-W8B6-1: pure logic in `pr_app::startup` (`options`, `ui_hop`, `settings_load`, `instance`, `early_decode`; `crash`, `recovery`, `shutdown`, `smoke`, `placement_read`), the pieces that name both crates in `pr_shell::startup` (`error`, `options`, `graph`, `state`, `instance`, `phase_one`; `run`, `window`, `overlay_vm`, `forward_target`, `recovery`, `smoke`, `shutdown`), two small native probes in `pr_platform_win::shell_startup` (`monitor_probe`, `local_time`). The plan's names `shell_startup::startup::{options, instance, settings}` and `{recovery, run, shutdown}` are NOT used (one crate cannot hold them: `pr-app` cannot name `pr-platform-win` types, D-W8B1-1). All module declarations in the three `mod.rs` indexes were added by the contract writer. | 17d, 17e |
| D-W8B6-21 | **No async: `StartupCoreAsync` is a step machine with one primitive, `ui_hop`.** The two `await` points (the settings/language load and the second-instance hand-off) become a worker that owns a `UiHopSender<T>` and a continuation registered on the UI thread (`thread_local!` registry keyed by an id; the job posted through `UiScheduler` finds it). A sender dropped unsent delivers `Err(HopLost)`, so a dead worker never leaves a window-less process holding the instance mutex (R2-F-04). `RunStartupGuardedAsync` becomes `error::catch_step` (`catch_unwind` around every UI-thread step: a panic is `StartupError::Panicked` and ends in `fail_startup`); this is one of the three last-resort panic catches R0003 allows on the UI thread (the others: the loop fault catch and `run_fail_startup`, D-W8B6-25). Every first-half path ends in exactly one call of `PhaseOneDone`. | 17d |
| D-W8B6-22 | **`ShellStartupContext` is built AFTER the load, from what is at hand.** The C# builds it before `await settingsLoad` from two shared `Task`s; Rust's `StartupTask` and `PrefetchHandle` are single-use. The settings member is a `StartupTask` that is already ready (a clone of the loaded settings), the placement member is the real handle when the early decode did not take it, else an EMPTY handle (`PrefetchHandle { handle: None }`, built inside `pr_app`, the field is `pub(crate)`). Nothing reads the field (feature packages and diagnostics, C-16). Alternative (open question 1 of 17d): amend `context.rs` so `placement` is `Option<WindowPlacementData>`. | 17d |
| D-W8B6-23 | **Single instance: acquire on the UI thread, hand off on a worker, decide with a pure table.** `InstanceGate` (pure trait in `pr_app::startup::instance`) is implemented over `InstanceScope` by `ScopeGate`; `try_take_lock` is the cheap call; the blocking `forward_to_owner` (up to 1.5 s) runs on a worker (`PhotoReview.Handoff`) and its result hops back; `handoff_decision(forwarded, mode)` is the C# `PerFolder ? FolderAlreadyOpen : AppAlreadyRunningNoResponse` table. The two boxes are information boxes through the host dialog service; both exit paths quit with code 0. `--smoke` creates no scope. The scope is created AFTER the settings (Q-R18) with prefix `INSTANCE_NAME_PREFIX` or the env override, `allow_server_foreground: true`, and its callback is the coalescer's `submit`. | 17d |
| D-W8B6-24 | **The shared graph (W8-09b) is a seam: `StartupGraph` struct + `GraphBuilder` closure** (same reasoning as `DialogServices`, D-W8B6-10: 09b's type names are not fixed). One field per `GetRequiredService<T>()` of `ShellStartup`: log, config and placement file paths, Explorer order provider, settings store, localization, preview service (as `PrewarmPreviews`), `ViewportSizeSource`, display clock, shared view model, journal, clock, `DialogServices`. `production_graph_builder()` is `todo!("W8-09b")`: the 09b port replaces the body (the one cross-unit edit of 09b); 17d and 17e tests use a fake builder over temp files. `GraphInputs` gives the builder the host services of 17c, the dispatcher, the loop, the hub, the log and the mark sink. `GlobalLog` (`Log` over `AppLog::global()`) is the `Arc<dyn Log>` used before the graph exists. | 17d |
| D-W8B6-25 | **Crash handling (P-15) in Rust.** (a) Dispatcher exceptions "marked handled": the dispatcher already catches and logs a panic of a posted job (D-W8B3H-10); a panic captured by a window procedure is resumed out of `MessageLoop::run` (D-W8B3H-4) and caught by `run` with `catch_unwind(AssertUnwindSafe(|| message_loop.run()))` (the one loop-level catch), logged FORCED (`AppLog::error_forced("UI loop exception", ..)`, even when logging is off, R2-F-12) and the loop runs again, up to `MAX_LOOP_FAULTS` (50), then exit code 1. (b) `AppDomain.UnhandledException` -> `std::panic::set_hook` chained to the previous hook, installed ONCE at the start of `run` (earlier than the C# line 321; the report is forced and independent of the settings; 17d never calls a 17e function), disabled by `install_process_hooks: false` in tests. (c) `UnobservedTaskException` has no Rust twin (no tasks; every worker catches and reports its own panic, R0003): `Different` row `X-startup-crash-handling`. (d) The last-resort catches allowed on the UI thread: `catch_step`, the loop catch, and `run_fail_startup`'s two guarded calls. | 17e |

### Wave 8 batch B6, group H3 (W8-20a, W8-20b): decisions, lead actions and cross-unit notes

Written by the contract writer of group H3. The lead merges the table into `contracts/README.md` ("Lead decisions (Wave 8 batch B6)") and may overrule a row. Numbers `D-W8B6-40..59` are the block reserved for H3 (H1 used 1..19, H2 20..39). Detail and reasons are in `contracts/W8-20a.md` and `contracts/W8-20b.md`. Where a contract question had a recommended default it was taken (standing instruction, P-45 style).

| # | Decision (acted on) | Unit |
|---|---|---|
| D-W8B6-40 | **Layering correction: the exe and every whole-shell test live in `pr-shell`, not `pr-app`.** The plan rows say `crates/pr-app/src/main.rs` (20a) and `crates/pr-app/tests/ui_smoke.rs` (20b). The merged layering (PR #91, D-W8B1-1) has `pr-platform-win -> pr-app` and `pr-shell -> both`; `ShellStartup::run` is in `pr-shell`, and `pr-app` cannot name it. So: `crates/pr-shell/src/main.rs` (already the `[[bin]] PhotoReviewRs`), `crates/pr-shell/src/entry.rs`, `crates/pr-shell/tests/{exe_process, exe_resources, entry_args, build_res_writer, ui_smoke*, ui_parity_map}.rs`. Restates D-W8B6-34 (17e). The plan row text is corrected by the lead (D-W8B6-52). | 20a, 20b |
| D-W8B6-41 | **The entry is split into four small functions and ends in `std::process::exit(i32)`.** `args_from_os` (skip the program name, lossy UTF-8), `run_main(os_args, run)` (decode, run, return the code unchanged), `production_run` (`ShellStartup::run` with `ShellStartupOptions::default()`), `main_entry() -> !`. The seam makes the argument and exit-code rules testable without a window. `ExitCode::from(u8)` is not used (256 would become 0). No `catch_unwind` in `main` (R0003 names three; a panic escaping `run` ends with 101, `Different`, D-W8B6-54). `main.rs` carries `windows_subsystem = "windows"` (C# `WinExe`): no console, so every failure of this unit logs forced through `AppLog`. | 20a |
| D-W8B6-42 | **Manifest and icon are embedded by a hand-written `.res` built in `build.rs`; no resource crate.** `build_support/res_writer.rs` (pure, std only, tested through `#[path]`) writes the empty first resource, `RT_MANIFEST` 1 (the text of `pr-platform-win/src/win32/shell.manifest`, one source of truth) and the `RT_ICON` images plus `RT_GROUP_ICON` 32512 of `assets/PhotoReviewRs.ico`; `cargo:rustc-link-arg-bin=PhotoReviewRs=<res>` (never `rustc-link-arg`, which would reach the test executables and the spike). This replaces the D-W8B3-8 recommendation (`/MANIFESTINPUT`) so one mechanism carries both resources; `/MANIFEST:NO` is the documented fix if the linker reports CVT1100. A missing icon asset is a build warning, not a failure. The icon is a synced asset: the lead adds `src/PhotoReview.App/Assets/PhotoReview.ico` -> `assets/PhotoReviewRs.ico` to `tools/sync-from-csharp.ps1` and runs it (372 526 bytes at `d92d6aae`); nobody copies it by hand. Closes the open questions of W8-13b Q3 and D-W8B3H-16 ("lead decision on the resource crate": none). | 20a |
| D-W8B6-43 | **Test drivers with `unsafe` live in `pr-platform-win` behind the existing `test-support` feature.** New modules `exe_resources` (reads the manifest and icon group of a built exe through `LoadLibraryEx(.., DATAFILE)`) and `ui_driver` (`PostMessageW` of key, wheel, mouse, command and close messages to a window of another thread, window discovery by owner, `close_main_window_of_process`), both `#[cfg(all(windows, feature = "test-support"))]`; `pr-shell` enables the feature in its `[dev-dependencies]`. Keeps AGENTS.md "`unsafe` only in the FFI crates" while the whole-shell tests, which need these calls, sit in `pr-shell`. No input is ever sent with `SendInput` or `SetForegroundWindow` (C# N-8). | 20a, 20b |
| D-W8B6-44 | **The process's main thread enters an STA apartment first (C# `[STAThread]`).** `pr_platform_win::shell_startup::apartment::StaApartment::enter()` = `CoInitializeEx(COINIT_APARTMENTTHREADED)`; `main_entry` calls it before anything else. Reason: `wic::interop::ensure_com_initialized` (P-25) puts a thread in the MTA, after which `OleInitialize`/`RegisterDragDrop` in `shell_input::drop_target` fails with `RPC_E_CHANGED_MODE`, and the Shell file and folder dialogs need an STA. `RPC_E_CHANGED_MODE` is a logged, non-fatal error. The W8-20b rig thread enters the same apartment (C# `ShellRun` used an STA thread). | 20a, 20b |
| D-W8B6-45 | **Tests of the exe and the rig: what is ignored, and the only timed waits.** `exe_resources`, `entry_args`, `build_res_writer`, `shell_startup_apartment` need no desktop and are NOT ignored; `exe_process` and every `ui_smoke*` test are `#[ignore = "native: needs desktop"]` (S3 thumb latency and S6 are `#[ignore = "manual: perf report"]`). Waits are `Condvar`/channel waits bounded at 60 s. The accepted timed ticks, each because no event exists from outside the thing waited on: 200 ms `recv_timeout` ticks in `exe_process` (the child's perf CSV grows; the child's main window exists), the 50 ms tick of `wait_for_owned_window` (a window of another class appeared), and the 250 ms switch interval of the S6 report; each tick is a timeout on a channel or condvar that the child's exit or a frame can cut short, never `thread::sleep`. | 20a, 20b |

#### Cross-unit notes
- **17e**: `ShellStartup::run` must stay callable from a spawned STA thread any number of times in one process (the rig starts one run per test): `install_process_hooks: false` and the `OnceLock` of the hook cover it; `Win32UiDispatcher::shutdown()` after the last step (17e semantics 1d) is what lets the next run in the same process create a new dispatcher; the abandoned-run guard of the rig exists because a run that never returns poisons the process-wide D3D/OLE state.
- **17c/09b**: `ShellServices::build` and the graph must not read `%LOCALAPPDATA%` when `AppPaths` is overridden (R0002); the 20a process tests set `PHOTOREVIEW_RS_DATA_ROOT` + `PHOTOREVIEW_RS_ISOLATE_CONFIG` and rely on that.
- **13b**: `ShellIcons::load` finds the icon in the exe's `RT_GROUP_ICON` 32512; in a test executable (no resource) it returns 0 and windows keep the default icon, which is why the window-level icon test uses the seams, the resource half is `exe_carries_the_application_icon_group`, and the LIVE half (W8-13b Q3: "the `ui_smoke` test asserts `load_big(96) != 0`") is an assertion of the 20a process test `exe_with_a_jpeg_draws_...` through `ui_driver::main_window_icons_of_process`, not of any `ui_smoke*` test (the rig runs in the test executable).
- **12d/13i**: the surface fault hooks (`DeviceResources::set_present_fault` / `set_end_draw_fault`) are already `pub`; what S2 lacks is the `Rc<MainWindow>` (see lead action 8, R2).
- **18o/18x**: S5 `the_shell_composition_root_runs_its_native_dialogs_inside_the_shell_loop` is the only test that proves the 18o router and the 17c factory in one process; it must run after 18o and 17c merge.
- **P-44**: C-14 (external editor) and the two P-44 dialogs stay `Pending`; when W8-18x lands, S0's table row C-14 changes its gate to `Native` with the new files.

#### Not done and out of scope here
- The production code of 20a (`entry.rs`, `build.rs`, `res_writer.rs`, `apartment.rs`, `exe_resources.rs`, `ui_driver.rs` are stubs), the rig and every test body of 20b (stubs with `todo!()`; the parity guard tests are stub-`#[ignore]`d so the workspace gate stays green).
- The oracle (not needed, D-W8B6-51), the shared graph (09b), the external-editor dialogs (P-44), the optional version resource, release packaging, the attended R0001 p95 re-measure.

### Wave 8 batch B6, group H4 (W8-09b: the shared service graph): decisions, lead actions and cross-unit notes

Written by the contract writer of W8-09b (`contracts/W8-09b.md`) AFTER the B6 reconciliation above, from the code on `master` (47ae13f). Numbers `D-W8B6-150..159` are the block reserved for H4; no earlier decision is changed (D-W8B6-10, -11, -15, -24, -47 and -106 are closed or corrected by the rows below, each saying which part). The contract has four independent slices (one PR each): S1 `composition::{texts, adapters}`, S2 five `pr-platform-win` adapters, S3 `composition::{platform, registry}`, S4 `composition::{main_view_model, graph}` plus the cross-unit edit of `startup/graph.rs`. Stubs compile (`cargo check -p pr-shell -p pr-platform-win --locked`); every body is `todo!()`. No oracle command: the golden `shell-composition-registrations` of W8-17c already lists the 36 registrations of `AddPhotoReviewShared` (indices 12 to 47).

| # | Decision (acted on) | Unit |
|---|---|---|
| D-W8B6-150 | **Scope: the localized text adapters belong to 09b.** Every library below `pr-app` takes an injected `*Texts` trait (D13/D41) and ships only an English implementation: `JournalTexts`, `FileActionTexts`, `UndoTexts`, `RecoveryRetryTexts`, `FileHashTexts`, `SourceBytesTexts`, `WebpHeicTexts`, `RawDecoderTexts`, `RawRoutingTexts`, `TurboJpegTexts`, `SettingsTexts` and the `TextSource` of the coordinators (W3-06 and W8-08a both say "the App composition root provides it"). No unit in the plan wrote them. `composition::texts::LocalizedTexts` is ONE zero-sized type implementing all twelve (74 + 2 methods) over `Localizer::current()` / `number_culture()` at every call (language switch applies at once). `SettingsTexts` uses it too, instead of 17d's `LocalizerSettingsTexts` (fixed localizer at construction) for the store built before the language is loaded. | 09b S1 |
| D-W8B6-151 | **Scope: five OS adapters belong to 09b (slice S2).** Found by grep at `47ae13f`: (a) `ExplorerOrderService` and `explorer_native_view::query_shell` are `pub(crate)` and the service has only `with_query`, so no code outside `pr-platform-win` can build the production provider: `explorer_order_provider::production_explorer_order(log)`; (b) no `PhysicalMemorySource` / `MemoryInfoSource` for Windows (C# `RamBudgetPolicy`, `MemoryHeadroom`): `physical_memory::WindowsPhysicalMemory`; (c) no `WindowsShellIntegration` (only the trait and the pure `shell_menu_command`): `shell_integration` (port of the 77-line C# class + its 6 native tests, private registry key); (d) no `CultureLookup`, installed UI culture, `utc_to_local` or `DateText` for Windows: `culture`; (e) `PreviewPayloadCodec` and `CacheImageReader` have no production implementation although `wic::cache_image` has the free functions: `wic::cache_adapters`. A sixth gap, the WIC `EmbeddedThumbnailBackend`, is NOT included (open question O1): `UnavailableEmbeddedThumbnails` stands in and `X-embedded-thumbnail-backend` is a `Different`/Todo row. The lead may split S2 into a W8-09c; the slices do not depend on each other. | 09b S2 |
| D-W8B6-152 | **The OS seams are ONE struct, `PlatformServices`** (`composition::platform`): 17 public fields (recycle bin, live-operation registry, memory probe, natural comparer, display clock, Explorer provider, physical memory, memory info, shell integration, payload codec, cache image reader, embedded thumbnails, fine scaler, LibRaw API, culture lookup, installed UI culture, platform codec), `PlatformServices::production(log, PlatformOverrides)` for the real ones. A test builds it field by field from fakes (no `Default`, a missing seam is a compile error: the Rust form of "fails loudly when missing"). `GraphOverrides::{recycle_bin, explorer_order}` reach `production` BEFORE the adapters are created (no Explorer pump thread, no real bin facade for nothing). The live-operation registry uses `INSTANCE_PREFIX` (`PhotoReviewRs`) instead of the C# default `PhotoReview` (R0002). | 09b S3 |
| D-W8B6-153 | **Bridges, and WHO fills `DialogServices`.** (a) `ShellServices` holds `Rc<Win32DialogService>`, `Rc<Win32ClipboardService>`, `Rc<Win32PresentationSinkFactory>`; `MainViewModelDeps` takes `Arc<dyn DialogService>`, `Box<dyn ClipboardService>` and the composition `PresentationSinkFactory`; the orphan rule forbids impls on `Rc<..>`, so `adapters::{SharedDialogService, SharedClipboard, Win32SinkFactoryAdapter}` (one-field newtypes; the sink adapter exists until 16i implements the trait itself, D-W8B4V-11 / A11). `Arc::new(SharedDialogService(..))` carries `#[allow(clippy::arc_with_non_send_sync)]` (UI thread only). (b) **Corrects D-W8B6-10 and D-W8B6-15 (c):** the builder of 09b fills `DialogServices` (17d's `StartupGraph::dialog_services` and its phase-one step 1 already bind it); 17e does nothing with the registry. The 14 field values are in the contract table; `update_checker` is `None` (the C# graph registers no `IUpdateChecker`), `decoder_factory` is a lazy wrapper, `benchmark_sessions` is `production_session_factory(BenchmarkSessionDeps { .. })` (closes 17c Q7). | 09b S1, S4 |
| D-W8B6-154 | **Lazy singletons, and the settings read order (the load-bearing rule).** C# DI created every singleton at its first `GetRequiredService`; `PreviewImageService` and `SourceBytesCachePolicy` read the settings THEN (capacity, RAM percent, preload window: "applies after a restart"), which in C# was after `store.Load()`. In Rust 17d builds the graph before the settings worker runs. So `SharedServices::build` creates nothing; every accessor is a `OnceLock` cell; `StartupGraph::previews` is `LazyPrewarmPreviews` and `DialogServices::decoder_factory` is `LazyDecoderFactory`. **Blocking item B1 (lead):** `StartupGraph::view_model` is an eager field, so building the view model at `servicesBuilt` resolves the preview service with DEFAULT settings. Recommended: amend 17d/17e so `view_model` is a factory called by 17e's `create_main_window` after `apply_loaded_settings` (about 5 lines in `graph.rs` and `window.rs`); fallback: the builder calls `settings_store.load(None)` itself (a second small read); rejected: accept the defaults. | 09b S3, S4 / 17d, 17e |
| D-W8B6-155 | **Blocking item B2: the preload scheduler cannot see the catalog.** `PreloadScheduler`'s `EntriesSnapshotFn` / `SnapshotVersionFn` are `Send + Sync` closures called on the UI thread when a run starts; the `ReviewCatalog` is owned by value by `MainViewModel` and is `!Sync`; nothing in `pr-app` publishes it. `CatalogFeed` (a shared cell: structural version + `Arc<[CatalogEntry]>` with stable identity) is what the closures read; it is filled through a NEW defaulted trait method `IPreloadController::catalog_changed(&self, &ReviewCatalog)` (amendment **A12**, pr-app, about 20 lines) called right before each of the four `preload_around` call sites (`present_a.rs` 615 and 630, `file_actions/undo.rs` 188, `viewmodels/main/notify_move.rs` 520-522). `FeedPreloadController` overrides it. Without A12 the preload sees an empty list (no preload). | 09b S4 / pr-app |
| D-W8B6-156 | **No oracle command.** The registrations golden exists (17c, 50 descriptors, two runs byte-identical per D-W8B6-102); 09b's pure logic is wiring whose expectations come from the C# tests. `shared_registrations_are_all_mapped` consumes golden indices 12 to 47 with a hand-written map (a registration missing from the map fails the test). | 09b |
| D-W8B6-157 | **Module homes.** Crate home `pr-shell` (D-W8B1-1, as 09a): `composition::{registry, main_view_model}` as the plan says, plus `platform`, `texts`, `adapters` (pure) and `graph` (`#[cfg(windows)]`, needs `startup`). OS adapters in `pr-platform-win` at crate level (`culture`, `explorer_order_provider`, `physical_memory`, `shell_integration`) and `wic::cache_adapters`. Plan row text "`composition::{registry, main_view_model}`" stays true; the row grows by the modules above. | 09b |
| D-W8B6-158 | **`GraphOverrides` / `production_graph_builder_with` are defined in `composition::graph` and re-exported by `startup::graph`** (closes the lead action of D-W8B6-47 / -106: the contract provides them, so 20b's amendment A1 is not needed). The six fields are exactly D-W8B6-47's. The ONE cross-unit edit of 09b in 17d's file is three lines: `production_graph_builder()` delegates to `production_graph_builder_with(GraphOverrides::default())`, and `pub use` of the two names. If B1 resolves as (a), two more lines in `startup/graph.rs` (field type) and `startup/window.rs`. | 09b S4 |
| D-W8B6-159 | **PARITY.** New rows `X-composition-registry`, `X-composition-main-view-model`, `X-composition-texts-adapters`, `X-platform-adapters` (status `Contract (W8-09b)`), `X-embedded-thumbnail-backend` (`Different`/Todo, O1). `Different` also: `AddPhotoReviewShared` null-argument and "missing host service" tests are unrepresentable (types); the Wpf-only registrations (`WpfKeyNameValidator`, `WpfClipboardService`, `WpfPresentationSinkFactory`) are retired. | 09b |
| D-W8B6-160 | **B1 approved (option a).** `StartupGraph::view_model` becomes a factory (`ViewModelFactory`) that W8-17e's `create_main_window` calls right after `apply_loaded_settings`, so the preview service reads the LOADED settings (capacity, RAM percent, preload window) when the view model is built; `build_shared_view_model` is the body of that factory. Cross-unit edit of 17d/17e files, done in slice S4 of W8-09b. Approved by the lead (Machine B session, 2026-10-22), following the recommendation in `contracts/W8-09b.md`. |
| D-W8B6-161 | **B2 approved (amendment A12 to pr-app).** `IPreloadController::catalog_changed(&self, &ReviewCatalog)` is added with a default no-op body and is called, with the catalog the caller already holds, immediately before each of the four `preload_around` call sites (`present_a.rs` x2, `undo.rs`, `notify_move.rs`); `FeedPreloadController` overrides it to publish the entries to the `CatalogFeed` (D-W8B6-155). Belongs to slice S4 of W8-09b. Approved by the lead (Machine B session, 2026-10-22), following the recommendation in `contracts/W8-09b.md`. |

## Wave 9 sync (EXTERNAL-EDITORS UI)

No unit is ported; no stub body is implemented.

### Unconfirmed sources
- The C# `FEAT-EXTERNAL-EDITORS.md` design describes the proposal for editor records, list editing, name suggestion, and shortcut/default-editor behaviour; it is marked unconfirmed in the C# repository. The contracts take behaviour from the implementation and its tests, not from the proposal. `ExternalEditors.Reconcile`, `ApplyLegacyPath` and `BuildArguments` decide repair, migration and quoting where the proposal is silent. No other code/design conflict is asserted without evidence.
- C# `FEAT-RENAME*.md` is also unconfirmed. `RenamePromptDialog`, F2 rename, the rename menu entry, `RenameFile` shortcut and rename localization are excluded (another lane).

### Wave 9 lane E decisions (D-W9E-1..12; P-48 in `docs/PROPOSED-DECISIONS.md`)
- **D-W9E-1 persisted settings (W9-E1).** List plus default id plus RAW switch with the v3 -> v4 migration, config version 4, `external_editor_path` kept as a mirror of the default editor's path (the frozen WPF build reads it). Alternatives: keep only the legacy path; materialise lazily after launch.
- **D-W9E-2 repair and launch arguments (W9-E1).** C# repair rules, cap 32, arguments longer than 4000 UTF-16 units reset, default falls back to the first valid editor, `{file}` replaced in one left-to-right pass with Windows argument escaping. Alternatives: reject the file; accept raw values.
- **D-W9E-3 product-name lookup (W9-E3).** OS version-resource lookup stays in `pr-platform-win` (`ExternalEditorProductName::suggest`, ProductName then FileDescription, the caller falls back to the file name). No oracle (machine dependent); the pure choice `first_non_blank` is a unit test.
- **D-W9E-4 dialog capability (W9-E2/E3/E8). CORRECTED.** The first draft said `Supports(ExternalEditors)` stays false until dispatch is wired. In C# it is `true` in the same commit (`Win32SecondaryWindowPort.Supports`); the optional members of `ISecondaryWindowPort` default to `None` only for ports that do not implement them. W9-E8 makes it `true` and updates the golden `secondary-window-supports`.
- **D-W9E-5 shortcut and duplicate rules (W9-E1/E7).** The default-editor key is optional (empty = disabled), part of `OPTIONAL_NAMES`, and takes part in the duplicate check; the router resolves it at step 12f with no modifier, independent of fullscreen (the shared no-image early return still applies); the command ignores key auto-repeat; enum value 30. The first draft put the `ShortcutMappings` field in E7: moved to E1 because persistence and validation need it first.
- **D-W9E-6 menu structure and command ids (W9-E6/E9).** Submenu in list order, ids `ExternalEditor.Open.<id>` / `ExternalEditor.Add` / `ExternalEditor.SetDefault`, separator `Separator.ExternalEditor`, the default is checked and alone carries the default-editor key (an unknown default id marks the first editor). With no editor the entry is the single command it was.
- **D-W9E-7 language catalogs (W9-E2/E4/E6/E8).** The new en/vi keys (34 at `7eaee2a2`) come from a separate asset synchronisation at the new baseline; the synced JSON is never hand-edited by these contracts. Tests that read texts are blocked on it.
- **D-W9E-8 unit order.** E1 (Core) first; then E2, E3, E5, E6, E7; then E4, E8, E9. Each downstream unit consumes compiled upstream stubs.
- **D-W9E-9 JSON `null` elements in `ExternalEditors` (W9-E1).** Implemented without a behavior difference: the reader drops null slots and records their count in transient `ReadNotes.null_external_editors` (following the existing `null_actions` representation). `Reconcile` consumes that note and reports a repair, matching C#; pre-v4 migration consumes it silently before normalization. The public list remains `Vec<ExternalEditor>`, and the note is never persisted. Covered by `settings_store::load_null_editor_slot_alone_reports_once_and_persists_the_repair` and `load_version3_null_editor_slot_is_consumed_by_silent_migration`.
- **D-W9E-10 where `ExternalEditorsEdit` lives (W9-E2).** `pr_platform_win::dialogs::native::external_editors_dialog`, as in C# (internal to `Shell.Win32`). The first draft put it in `pr-core` and added a `show_external_editors` method to `pr_core::abstractions::DialogService`; the C# `IDialogService` has only the two `Prompt*` methods, so the third was invented and is removed.
- **D-W9E-11 the `SecondaryWindowDialogs` seam grows (W9-E8).** Five additive members with default bodies (`settings_with_editors`, `external_editors`, `pick_executable`, `text_prompt`, `choice_list`) and an `ExternalEditorsOpener` alias, so the W8-18o fakes keep compiling. Alternative: change `settings(...)` itself and update the W8-18o tests (the porter may fold `settings_with_editors` into `settings` once nothing depends on the old signature).
- **D-W9E-12 launcher seam (W9-E5).** `ExternalEditorLauncher::launch` gets a third `command_line` argument (C# `Action<string,string,string>`); the production launcher passes it with `raw_arg` and uses the editor's folder as the working directory. The merged test that asserted "one argument without a shell" is renamed: the contract is now the C# command-line text.

### Needs a human or product decision (acted on as recommended, P-48)
1. Move the spec baseline in `docs/SYNC-LEDGER.md` to `7eaee2a2` and regenerate the existing goldens (settings-*, shortcut-canonical, secondary-window-supports, settings-page-*): the diff must contain only version 4, three new keys, one more shortcut, plus the FEAT-RENAME effects.
2. Run the catalog sync (en/vi, 34 keys at `7eaee2a2`, about 29 for this lane) before porting E2, E4, E6, E8.
3. Order of the two lanes (external editors, rename): they edit the same files on disjoint lines; decide which merges first (the enum value 31 of `RenameFile` and the config version 4 are identical in both).
4. D-W9E-9 resolved by the transient read-note representation above; no product behavior change.
5. Whether the native dialogs of W9-E2/E3 may be ported before W8-18p/18q merge (they need `DialogMeasure`, `DialogWarningSink` and `FileFilterText`); the plan says no.

### Not verified
The three new oracle commands were run twice at `7eaee2a2` with byte-identical goldens. No oracle exists for E2, E3, E4, E5, E8 and E9. The mutation runs, the native tests and the desktop run are not done. The en/vi catalog keys are not synced. No unit is ported.

### Decisions

| ID | Decision |
|---|---|
| D-W9R-1 | The name policy and JPEG+RAW+XMP plan are pure `pr_core::file_actions::rename` APIs; string comparison follows C# OrdinalIgnoreCase, but name bytes and extension spelling are preserved. |
| D-W9R-2 | Rename is a same-folder Move using the existing journal schema and service gate. The Rust operation is blocking on the file-action lane per R0003; no new async runtime or `FileOperationType` variant. |
| D-W9R-3 | Undo, reconcile and recovery use stored spelling for case-only pairs; a third spelling is ambiguous and stays recoverable. |
| D-W9R-4 | Catalog rekey is atomic and in place; preserve index/metadata, bump one structural version, invalidate old-path caches. |
| D-W9R-5 | Controller order is validation, generation stop, preload cancel, worker I/O, catalog/session/presenter/cache rekey, preload restart; same name changes nothing. |
| D-W9R-6 | Retarget displayed state without decoding when possible. MainViewModel methods mutate and return `ChangeSet`; off-thread completion uses existing event plumbing. |
| D-W9R-7 | Menu item is Win32-only, appended as ID 18; F2 is optional default and an Action shortcut wins collision. Prompt validation checks catalog while typing and disk only on commit. |
| D-W9R-8 | Native rename dialog is separate from `TextPromptDialog` (the latter belongs to FEAT-EXTERNAL-EDITORS). F2 and right-click menu use one command path. |
| D-W9R-9 | Core fault matrix is a separate test owner because the C# test file exceeds a production slice; no real Recycle Bin operation. |
| D-W9R-10 | Large controller/presenter/view-model test suites are separate test owners; deterministic fakes replace sleeps. |
| D-W9R-11 | Native dialog and shell end-to-end tests remain `Partial` until an attended/hidden desktop run, regardless of cargo check. |
| D-W9R-12 | **Needs a human decision:** C# `RenameNamePolicy.Check` throws on a blank current path; Rust returns `RenameNameProblem::Empty` instead of panicking (default chosen, change only if a hard error type is wanted). |
| D-W9R-13 | Merge order resolved: lane E merged first. `RenameFile` stays last after `OpenInDefaultEditor`; `ReviewCommandType::RenameFile = 31`. |
| D-W9R-14 | Golden-pinned settings cases stay at `d92d6aae` until the lead advances `SYNC-LEDGER`; R7 updates rename expectations by hand. |

### Unconfirmed sources

For every item here the binding decision is **the C# code as implemented at `7eaee2a2` is the spec**. The older document at `57b04243` is a design rationale and was never confirmed as a behavior override.

| Design item or open question | Implemented C# resolution / divergence |
|---|---|
| Q1 case-only rename | Supported with `CaseOnlyRename` and stored-name enumeration; no temporary file name. |
| Q2 F2 default | `ShortcutMappings.RenameFile = "F2"`, optional/clearable; conflicting Action wins. |
| Q3 single Undo slot | Rename registers as Move and replaces the latest Undo action. |
| Q4 sidecar scope | JPEG+RAW group with known `.xmp` only. PR4 sidecar discovery is absent; do not invent `.dop`, `.pp3`, `.acr` support. |
| Q5 UI scope | **Divergence:** design recommends both Win32 and WPF, PR3 implements Win32 only. `ContextMenuItemInfo.ShellOnly` hides it from WPF and WPF settings preserves the hidden choice. |
| Q6 location after rename | Preserve index and viewer state; no sort or folder reload. |
| Q7 untouched failure | Journal line is Dismissed when unchanged is proven; ambiguous mutation stays Failed. |
| Design R36 title bar path was not checked | Implemented presenter/session notification and title propagation are the spec; tests must check displayed name rather than assume the old design inventory. |
| Manifest `longPathAware` was not checked | Implemented `RenameNamePolicy.Check` defaults to `longPathAware=false`; reject a new path reaching 260 only when longer than the existing one. |
| LibRaw file share mode was not checked | No claim that native RAW decode permits rename; cancel ZoomDetail pending work and require native verification. |
| Failure message for file locked by an external editor | **Divergence:** design suggests text telling user to close the editor; PR3 shows the generic `status.renameFailed` message on status bar, without an error dialog or special close-editor hint. |
| Preview cache re-key was optional in design | **Divergence:** code evicts old-path cache and original dimensions; it does not re-key decoded RAM image under the new name. |
| Design estimated four PRs | Implemented FEAT-RENAME commits are design, core, state and UI; the planned PR4 advanced sidecar discovery is absent. |
| Design says no code until confirmation | Historical statement is obsolete after #527/#529/#530. Contract source is implemented code, status label remains unconfirmed. |

## Wave 10 (contracts only: manual update HTTP, release, benchmark parity, RAW/native residual)

Merged from the four lane drafts (`contracts/drafts-w10-*.md`, removed in the same change). Decision numbers D-W10http-n, D-W10release-n, D-W10BENCH-n, D-W10RAW-n are kept. Nothing here is implemented; every decision is open for the lead unless a row says otherwise. Plans: `docs/WAVE-10-PLAN-http.md`.


### Wave 10 HTTP lane (W10-H1..H3: manual update transport)

#### D-W10http-1: OS stack = WinHTTP through `windows` / windows-rs

The C# baseline creates `new HttpClient()` without a handler, gives it `Timeout.InfiniteTimeSpan`, and uses a linked token with `CancelAfter(10 s)` (`UpdateChecker.cs` 21-45). `PhotoReview.Shell.Win32.csproj` targets `net10.0-windows` (7-10). Its application code does not set the handler's proxy, redirect, TLS, decompression, or HTTP version options. .NET 10's `HttpClientHandler` uses SocketsHttpHandler; `HttpClient.DefaultProxy` on Windows checks `HTTPS_PROXY`/`ALL_PROXY` (and `NO_PROXY`) before the current user's proxy settings, including PAC/WPAD. This is a runtime default, not code in the repository. [DefaultProxy](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/system.net.http.httpclient.defaultproxy), [HttpClientHandler](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/fundamentals/runtime-libraries/system-net-http-httpclienthandler).

| Stack | Proxy and trust parity | Unsafe / cancellation | Binary size and fake tests |
|---|---|---|---|
| **WinHTTP (chosen)** | Windows certificate validation through the OS; explicitly implement .NET environment precedence and current-user WinINet/IE proxy settings with `WinHttpGetIEProxyConfigForCurrentUser` and PAC/WPAD via `WinHttpGetProxyForUrl`. WinHTTP's `PRECONFIG` alone is insufficient. Redirect policy and decompression need explicit settings. | Small FFI surface confined to `pr-platform-win`, every `unsafe` block justified. Use asynchronous request handles and close the **request** handle on cancel/deadline; completion races must be guarded. `WinHttpSetTimeouts` is phase protection, not the whole-exchange deadline. | Windows DLL, no new Rust HTTP dependency; pure policy and in-process loopback tests. |
| WinINet | Current-user proxy is close, but env precedence still manual; Windows trust. Intended for interactive clients, with more implicit state. | WinINet FFI and handle-cancel races; verify prompt cancellation before adopting. | OS DLL; fake protocol tests possible but global configuration harder to isolate. |
| Windows.Web.Http / WinRT | Windows trust and brokered settings, but .NET proxy and redirect defaults require measurement. | COM/WinRT lifetime and async cancellation mapping are larger than needed. | OS runtime; testing adapter without network is more complex. |
| Pure Rust HTTP client | New dependency plus explicit Windows trust and proxy/PAC integration; cannot assume parity from TLS backend alone. | Smaller local unsafe surface; cancellation depends on client. | Adds code and dependency tree; fake servers straightforward. |
| `curl.exe` / PowerShell child | External executable's proxy, TLS, redirects, headers and decompression differ by installation/configuration. | Kill child on cancellation; process cleanup and output limits. | No linked crate, but startup and distribution costs; difficult deterministic tests. |

WinHTTP is chosen because the Windows OS supplies certificate validation and PAC primitives without a new third-party crate. The porter must reproduce the documented .NET defaults explicitly, never assume WinHTTP and SocketsHttpHandler are interchangeable. [WinHTTP current-user proxy](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/winhttp/setting-wininet-proxy-configurations-in-winhttp), [WinHTTP cancellation](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/winhttp/concurrency-in-winhttp).

#### D-W10http-2: preserve the existing checker and UI logic

Inject `Arc<UpdateChecker>` built from `Arc<WinHttpTransport>` into the existing `DialogServices.update_checker`. The C# presenter falls back to `new UpdateChecker()` when DI supplies none (`SettingsPresenter.cs` 580 at `7eaee2a2`; 575 at `d92d6aae`); the Rust presenter has no such fallback. Keep the merged UI worker/poster, localized status mapping, allow-listed URL validation and close cancellation. Only fix demonstrated gaps, with a fake or native test. Do not introduce another network request or an automatic check.

#### D-W10http-3: HTTPS proxy source precedence (recommended, believed, not verified)

For an HTTPS target, choose a usable nonempty `HTTPS_PROXY`, else `ALL_PROXY`, else the current user's WinINet/IE configuration, else direct. Apply `NO_PROXY` or the user's bypass list after selecting the source. For current-user automatic configuration, call `WinHttpGetIEProxyConfigForCurrentUser` and resolve PAC/WPAD for the target with `WinHttpGetProxyForUrl`; WinHTTP machine `PRECONFIG` alone does not reproduce `HttpClient.DefaultProxy`. This is the recommended implementation rule, **believed but not verified** for empty/invalid environment values, bypass grammar and PAC edge cases. It follows the documented Windows `HttpClient.DefaultProxy` source order but is not yet a measured baseline result. [DefaultProxy](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/system.net.http.httpclient.defaultproxy), [WinHTTP AutoProxy](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/winhttp/winhttp-autoproxy-api).

Lead verification probe: proposed `tools/oracle-cs/Commands/W10-H1.cs` should include a tiny .NET 10 command that prints `HttpClient.DefaultProxy.GetProxy` and `IsBypassed` for an HTTPS test URL under controlled `HTTPS_PROXY`, `ALL_PROXY`, `NO_PROXY`, and current-user proxy scenarios, plus the default values of `HttpClientHandler.AllowAutoRedirect`, `MaxAutomaticRedirections`, `AutomaticDecompression`, `MaxResponseHeadersLength`, and `HttpRequestMessage.Version`. Run it only in a controlled environment; the file and probe are **proposed only, not created or run** by this writer. PAC/WPAD requires a separately controlled local PAC endpoint.

#### Roster

| WU | Unit | Contract | Test kind | Batch order |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| W10-H1 | HTTP redirect/proxy/deadline/error policy | `contracts/W10-H1.md` | F | H1 |
| W10-H2 | WinHTTP transport and streamed body | `contracts/W10-H2.md` | F/N | H2 after H1 |
| W10-H3 | Shell injection and manual check completion | `contracts/W10-H3.md` | F/N | H3 after H2 |

Plan: `docs/WAVE-10-PLAN-http.md`.

#### Runtime defaults and uncertainties

Verified from Microsoft documentation: automatic redirects are enabled with a maximum of 50; .NET 5+ rejects automatic HTTPS-to-HTTP downgrade; `Authorization` is cleared on automatic redirect. [Redirects](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/system.net.http.httpclienthandler.allowautoredirect), [limit](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/system.net.http.httpclienthandler.maxautomaticredirections). C# `UpdateChecker.Classify` maps `NameResolutionError`, `ConnectionError`, `ProxyTunnelError`, and unknown to Offline, all other listed `HttpRequestError` values to BadResponse (`UpdateChecker.cs` 74-83).

The request is explicitly a `new HttpRequestMessage(...)`, whose default version is HTTP/1.1; `HttpClient.DefaultRequestVersion` does not override `SendAsync`'s request object. The default response-header limit is 64 KiB. The normal `HttpClient.Timeout` is 100 seconds but the C# checker explicitly replaces it with `Timeout.InfiniteTimeSpan` and a linked token canceled after ten seconds. [Request version](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/system.net.http.httprequestmessage.version), [SendAsync version rule](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/system.net.http.httpclient.defaultrequestversion), [header limit](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/fundamentals/code-analysis/quality-rules/ca2262), [timeout](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/system.net.http.httpclient.timeout).

**Not verified:** the exact .NET 10 default `AutomaticDecompression` value (the public page states `None` only for .NET Framework 4.x), request version policy, Windows trust store scope, exact redirect status/method edge cases, exact WinHTTP error-code mapping, whether proxy auto-discovery time is included in the WinHTTP deadline, and repository-wide absence of an external `HttpClient.DefaultProxy` setter. Probe these locally or inspect pinned runtime sources before implementation; do not encode guessed values as parity. The local tests must not call the internet.

### Wave 10 benchmark lane (W10-B1..B3)

| WU | Unit | Color | Porter | Depends |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| W10-B1 | Benchmark comparison harness | 🟡 | Sonnet 5.5 | W7-01a, W7-01b, W7-03a, W7-03c, W7-03d; engine-backed backend wiring |
| W10-B2 | Attended production re-measure protocol | 🟡 | Manual operator | W8-12d, W8-12e, W8-12h, W8-16a; attended session |
| W10-B3 | Phase 7 parity report format | 🟡 | Sonnet 5.5 | W10-B1, W10-B2 |

#### Decisions D-W10BENCH

- **D-W10BENCH-1 — Proposed numeric parity rule.** Rule: compare paired repeats by index and use medians; `allowed=max(5%*|B|,1 ms)`, `d=R-B`; below `-allowed` Improvement, `[-allowed,0]` Pass, `(0,allowed]` WithinNoise, above allowed Regression only when a strict majority of paired differences exceed allowed, otherwise WithinNoise/unstable. Only p95 Regression fails exit code 1; p50/max Regression is a warning. Rationale: explicit Phase 7 gate using C# PerfComparer defaults. Who may overrule: project lead by recorded decision.
- **D-W10BENCH-2 — Sequential same-machine runs.** Rule: C# and Rust never run concurrently and use the same fixture, profile list, display/power conditions, and separate output folders. Rationale: avoid resource contention and input drift. Who may overrule: project lead with an ADR describing the controlled parallel setup.
- **D-W10BENCH-3 — Comparison preconditions.** Rule: no parity claim before Rust engine-backed backend wiring and schema parity. Rationale: `main.rs` currently selects `UnwiredBackend`, which cannot provide valid batch results. Who may overrule: none for a claim; implementation can change the backend but must meet the same evidence gate.
- **D-W10BENCH-4 — Conditional UI experiment gate.** Rule: W8-12j is started or canceled using W8-12j's attended keep-warm-on trigger and repeat evidence; B1 batch numbers do not replace it. Rationale: benchmark workload and production frame stalls are different measures. Who may overrule: project lead by ADR.
- **D-W10BENCH-5 — Metrics are diagnostic.** Rule: every `ReviewMetricsSnapshot` value is reported as C#, Rust, and delta and never fails parity; Metrics presence and key shape remain schema-exact. Rationale: timing and scheduling can legitimately change counters. Who may overrule: project lead by ADR.
- **D-W10BENCH-6 — P0 production measurement driver.** Rule: production p95 requires `PhotoReviewRs.exe` plus frame log and automated 250 ms Right-arrow driver, or an updated production-surface spike; old spike subset is indicative only. Rationale: old spike lacks keep-warm and is not production surface. Who may overrule: project lead only after equivalent production evidence is documented.

#### Preconditions

- Engine-backed benchmark backend wired in `main.rs` (not yet done).
- W8-12d, W8-12e, and W8-12h merged before the attended production measurement.
- P0 measurement driver exists; no suitable input driver was found and it is to be added by a later unit.
- W8-16a frame logging available; `PHOTOREVIEW_RS_FRAME_LOG` accepts a file path.
- Attended session with AC power, display setup, IME checks, and fixture generation.

#### Open questions

- Confirm the B1 JSON envelope against its public API when implementation begins.

### Wave 10 RAW/native lane (W10-N1..N4)

Roster:

| WU | Unit | Rust area | Porter | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| W10-N1 | Stage pinned native DLLs | `native/x64/` staging procedure | Sonnet | Hash verification; no committed DLLs |
| W10-N2 | Execute native tests | `pr-libraw`, `pr-turbojpeg` ignored native tests | Sonnet | Windows x64, pinned DLLs, timeout |
| W10-N3 | RAW corpus and strict run | `pr-test-support::raw`, RAW integration tests | Sonnet | Consent, manifest hashes, strict mode |
| W10-N4 | Wave 5 mutation survivors | W5 Rust test suites | Sonnet | Identity-reconciled mutation evidence |

Decisions D-W10RAW-1..4 (proposed; open for the lead):

- **D-W10RAW-1 — DLLs stay local and pinned.** Stage only SHA-256-verified C# baseline DLLs under Rust `native/x64/`; never commit DLLs. Network fetch requires owner consent.
- **D-W10RAW-2 — Native run is desktop/manual.** Run ignored LibRaw/TurboJPEG suites on Windows x64 with explicit timeout and serial test threads. Regular CI does not execute them unless it stages verified binaries.
- **D-W10RAW-3 — Corpus fetch requires consent.** The approximately 590 MB CC0 corpus remains absent until the owner explicitly approves the fetch; strict mode makes missing samples fail.
- **D-W10RAW-4 — Mutation status follows identities.** Preserve caught/equivalent/unreconstructed distinctions; do not combine overlapping counts or describe partial scans as complete.

References: contracts/W10-N1.md through W10-N4.md. Hash pins and limitations are transcribed there.
